u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
, which satisfies
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
Denote

(4.1)

M_{0}:=\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{C(\mathbb{T})},\quad\lambda_{0}:=|X_{0}|_{*},\quad Q_{0}:=\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}.

Clearly,
0<\lambda_{0}\leq\inf_{s\in\mathbb{T}}|X_{0}^{\prime}(s)|\leq M_{0}
.
Here we put a second term in the definition of
Q_{0}
just to avoid possible issues in the special case
u_{0}=0
.

Fix

(4.2)

\gamma\in\left[\frac{2}{p},1\right)\cap\left(1-\frac{2}{p},1\right).

In particular
\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
.
Let

(4.3)

\rho(t)=\rho_{X_{0},\gamma}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}.

This is well-defined since
\tau^{\gamma}\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}
, with
C
depending on
\gamma
only.

\rho(t)
is continuous and increasing on
(0,+\infty)
.
Moreover,
\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\rho(t)=0
.
Indeed,

(4.4)

\begin{split}\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq&\;\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\sqrt{\tau}}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+\tau^{\gamma}\Big\|\big(e^{-\frac{\sqrt{\tau}}{4}\Lambda}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}\big)X_{0}^{\prime}\Big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\tau^{\gamma/2}\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}+C\Big\|\big(e^{-\frac{\sqrt{\tau}-\tau}{4}\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)X_{0}^{\prime}\Big\|_{C(\mathbb{T})},\end{split}

which implies
\lim_{\tau\to 0^{+}}\tau^{\gamma}\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}=0
.

Define

(4.5)

\phi(t)=\phi_{X_{0}}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\big\|X_{0}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}.

It is also clear that
\phi(t)
is continuous and increasing on
[0,+\infty)
, and
\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\phi(t)=0
.

With
T\in(0,1)
and
Q>0
to be determined, we define

(4.6)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}:=&\;\Big\{u(x,t)\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))\cap C_{loc}((0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}):\\
&\;\qquad\quad\mathrm{div}\,u=0\mbox{ for all }t\in[0,T],\;\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Q,\\
&\;\qquad\quad\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\cdot,\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Q,\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}(\nu\eta)^{\gamma}\|u(\cdot,\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Q,\\
&\;\qquad\quad\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sup_{0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T}(\nu t_{1})^{\gamma}\cdot\frac{|u(x,t_{1})-u(x,t_{2})|}{(\nu(t_{1}-t_{2}))^{\gamma/2}}\leq Q\bigg\}.\end{split}

With
N>0
to be determined, and
\sigma:=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p})
, we let

(4.7)

\begin{split}\mathcal{X}:=&\;\Big\{X=X(s,t)\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\mathbb{T}))\cap C^{1}_{loc}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T}):\;X(s,0)=X_{0}(s),\\
&\;\qquad\quad t^{\gamma}\|X(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+\|X(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{C_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\big(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\big)\mbox{ for all }t\in(0,T],\\
&\;\qquad\quad\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\cdot,\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X(\cdot,\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq N\Big\}.\end{split}

Remark 4.1
.

The form of the bounds
2(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma})
in the definition of
\mathcal{X}
will be clear below; see (
4.35
) and (
4.36
).
Actually, one may take
\sigma
to be an arbitrary number in
(0,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p})
.
This fact will be used in the proof of Proposition
5.2
below.

Remark 4.2
.

In fact, in the definition of
\mathcal{X}
, the time-continuity of
X\in\mathcal{X}
in the
C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
-topology on
[0,T]
is guaranteed by the quantitative estimates in (
4.7
).
Indeed, the time-continuity of
X\in\mathcal{X}
at
t=0
is a consequence of the bound for
\|X(t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{C_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}
and the fact
\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\rho(t)=0
, while the time-continuity at
t\in(0,T]
follows from the interpolation inequalities that are similar to (
3.42
) and (
3.43
).

As a corollary,
t\mapsto X(t)
is continuous on
(0,T]
in the
C^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{T})
-topology for any
\beta\in(0,\gamma)
.
Indeed, this follows from the (local)
\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}
-bound on
X(t)
and the time-continuity of
X
in the
C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
-topology.

The main result of this section is as follows.

Proposition 4.1
.

Suppose
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
,
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, and they satisfy that
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.

Let
M_{0}
,
\lambda_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
be defined as in (
4.1
).
Fix
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)\cap(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
, and define
\rho=\rho(t)
as in (
4.3
).
Take
Q,\,N>0
as in (
4.24
) and (
4.42
) below respectively.
Then for some
T>0
, which depends on
\gamma,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda_{0},\,Q_{0},\,M_{0}
and
X_{0}
, there exists a unique
(u,X)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
satisfying (
2.18
) and (
2.19
) for all
t\in[0,T]
, where
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{X}
are defined as in (
4.6
) and (
4.7
), respectively.
In addition,

(1)

it holds pointwise on
(0,T)\times\mathbb{T}
that

(4.8)

\partial_{t}X=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda X+g_{X}+\big[h_{X}^{\nu}+e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X(t)})+B_{\nu}[u]\big]\circ X=u\big(X(s,t),t\big);

(2)

\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 2M_{0},\quad\inf_{t\in[0,T]}|X(t)|_{*}\geq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2},

and for any
\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,\gamma]
,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma^{\prime}}\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 4M_{0}.

In particular,
(u,X)
is a mild solution in the sense of Definition
2.1
to (
1.7
)-(
1.10
) on
[0,T]
with the initial condition (
1.11
).

Remark 4.3
.

The short-time characterizations of the mild solution in Theorem
2.1
follow by taking e.g.,
\gamma:=\max(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p},1-\frac{1}{p})
and
\gamma^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{p}
.

Remark 4.4
.

As is mentioned before, the
C^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
-regularity is critical for
X
, so it is natural that
T
depends implicitly on
X_{0}
, but not just on its
C^{1}
-seminorm
M_{0}
and its well-stretched constant
\lambda_{0}
.
We point out that such implicit constraints on
T
arise in (and only in) (
4.14
), (
4.21
), (
4.23
), (
4.34
)-(
4.36
), and (
4.51
) below.
They essentially require
(\rho_{X_{0},\gamma}(T)+T^{\sigma})
and
\phi_{X_{0}}(T)
to be smaller than certain constants that depend on
\gamma,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda_{0},\,Q_{0}
, and
M_{0}
.
Observe that the mappings
X_{0}\mapsto\rho_{X_{0},\gamma}
and
X_{0}\mapsto\phi_{X_{0}}
are continuous from
C^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
to
C([0,+\infty))
, so
T
can be made to depend continuously on
X_{0}
in the
C^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
-topology.
This fact will be used in Section
8
.

Remark 4.5
.

At this moment, we only claim the uniqueness of such
(u,X)
in
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
, but not the uniqueness of the mild solution.
The latter will be addressed in Proposition
5.1
.

We will prove Proposition
4.1
by a fixed-point argument.
We start by introducing the metrics on
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{X}
.

For
u,v\in\mathcal{U}
, define

(4.9)

d_{\mathcal{U}}(u,v):=\sup_{t\in(0,T]}(\nu t)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\|(u-v)(\cdot,t)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
denotes the Morrey norm on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
defined in (
2.1
).
Since
\|f\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\|f\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{2}{1-\gamma}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
where
\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\in(p,\infty)
, one can show that
d_{\mathcal{U}}(u,v)
is a well-defined metric on
\mathcal{U}
by interpolation.

For
X,Y\in\mathcal{X}
, we let

(4.10)

\begin{split}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X,Y):=&\;\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|(X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime})(\cdot,\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}(X-Y)(\cdot,\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}(X-Y)(\cdot,\eta)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}.\end{split}

Given the definition of
\mathcal{X}
, this is a well-defined metric on
\mathcal{X}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
equipped with the metric

(4.11)

d_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}}((u,X),(v,Y)):=d_{\mathcal{U}}(u,v)+d_{\mathcal{X}}(X,Y)

is a complete metric space.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
(\mathcal{U},d_{\mathcal{U}})
and
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}})
are both complete.
This stems from the compactness provided by the regularity assumptions on the elements in
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{X}
.

Take a Cauchy sequence
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\subset\mathcal{U}
under the metric
d_{\mathcal{U}}
.
By definition (
4.6
),
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, and it is also uniformly locally Hölder continuous in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(0,T]
.
By Arzelà-Ascoli theorem and Banach-Alaoglu theorem,
there exists a subsequence
\{u_{n_{k}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and
u_{*}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that, as
k\to+\infty
,
u_{n_{k}}
converges to
u_{*}
in the weak-
*
topology of
L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, and also converges to
u_{*}
locally uniformly in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times(0,T]
.
It is then straightforward to verify that
u_{*}\in\mathcal{U}
.

Next we show that
u_{n}\to u_{*}
as
n\to+\infty
under the metric
d_{\mathcal{U}}
.
For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
t\in(0,T]
and
r>0
, by the dominated convergence theorem,

\displaystyle r^{-1-\gamma}\int_{B(x,r)}|u_{n_{k}}-u_{*}|(y,t)\,dy=

\displaystyle\;\lim_{l\to+\infty}r^{-1-\gamma}\int_{B(x,r)}|u_{n_{k}}-u_{n_{l}}|(y,t)\,dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\limsup_{l\to+\infty}\|(u_{n_{k}}-u_{n_{l}})(\cdot,t)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

This implies

d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{n_{k}},u_{*})\leq\limsup_{l\to+\infty}d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{n_{k}},u_{n_{l}}),

and thus
\lim_{k\to+\infty}d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{n_{k}},u_{*})=0
.
Since
\{u_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a Cauchy sequence under the metric
d_{\mathcal{U}}
, the whole sequence must converge to
u_{*}\in\mathcal{U}
.
This shows that
(\mathcal{U},d_{\mathcal{U}})
is a complete metric space.

Now take a Cauchy sequence
\{X_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\subset\mathcal{X}
under the metric
d_{\mathcal{X}}
.
Let
X_{*}
be the
C_{loc}^{1}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T})
-limit of
\{X_{n}\}
; indeed, the definition of
d_{\mathcal{X}}
guarantees that this limit is well-defined.
We additionally define
X_{*}(s,0)=X_{0}(s)
, and let
X_{*}(s,T)
be the
C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
-limit of
X_{*}(\cdot,t)
as
t\to T^{-}
.
Note that the well-definedness of
X_{*}(s,T)
and the time-continuity of
X_{*}
in the
C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
-topology is implied by the quantitative estimates for
X_{*}
(see Remark
4.2
).
It is then not difficult to verify that
X_{*}\in\mathcal{X}
, and
d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{n},X_{*})\to 0
as
n\to+\infty
.
Therefore,
(\mathcal{X},d_{\mathcal{X}})
is complete.
∎

Given
(u,X)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
, using the notations in Section
2.2
, we let (cf. (
2.18
))

(4.12)

v(t):=u_{X}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}+B_{\nu}[u](t).

and (cf. (
2.19
))

(4.13)

Y(t):=e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}+\mathcal{I}\big[W[u,X;\nu,u_{0}]\big](t),

where
W
and
\mathcal{I}[W]
were defined in (
2.20
) and (
2.21
), respectively.
Define the solution mapping

\mathcal{T}:\,(u,X)\mapsto(v,Y).

In what follows, we will show that
\mathcal{T}
is a contraction mapping from
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
to itself, provided that
T
,

Q
, and
N
are suitably chosen.
This gives a fixed-point of
\mathcal{T}
, and thus a local mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) in the sense of Definition
2.1
.

4.2.
Bounds for the mapping
\mathcal{T}

In this subsection, we will show that by choosing
T
,
Q
, and
N
suitably,
\mathcal{T}
maps
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
into itself.

Take an arbitrary
(u,X)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
.
For
t\in[0,T]
,

\|X(\cdot,t)-X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|X(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+\|X_{0}-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}.

Recall that
\rho=\rho(t)
and
\phi=\phi(t)
were defined in (
4.3
) and (
4.5
), respectively.
We choose
T\in(0,1)
sufficiently small, which depends on
X_{0}
,
\gamma
, and
\lambda_{0}
, such that

(4.14)

2\big(\rho(T)+T^{\sigma}\big)\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{16},\quad\mbox{and}\quad\phi(T)\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{16}.

As a result, by the preceding estimate and (
4.7
), for all
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.15)

\|X(\cdot,t)-X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{8},

which implies

(4.16)

|X(\cdot,t)|_{*}\geq|X_{0}|_{*}-\frac{\lambda_{0}}{8}\geq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}=:\lambda.

In view of (
3.1
) and the definition of
\mathcal{X}
in (
4.7
),
\mathcal{X}\subset O_{T}^{\lambda}
with
\lambda=\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2}
.
We also have the simple bounds

(4.17)

\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq M_{0}+\frac{\lambda_{0}}{8}\leq 2M_{0},\quad\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{16}\leq\frac{M_{0}}{16},

and by interpolation, for all
\gamma^{\prime}\in[0,\gamma]
,

(4.18)

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma^{\prime}}\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)^{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{\gamma}}\big(2\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L_{s}^{\infty}}\big)^{1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{\gamma}}\leq 4M_{0}.

Moreover, by (
4.7
), for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]
,

\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{1})-X^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{4}=\frac{\lambda}{2}.

Let
(v,Y):=\mathcal{T}(u,X)
.
We start from estimating
v
.
By (
4.12
), (
4.17
), Lemma
3.2
, and (
3.8
) in Lemma
3.8
, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(4.19)

\begin{split}\|v(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq&\;\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\leq&\;C\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{2}}^{\frac{4}{p}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2-\frac{4}{p}}+\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\\
&\;+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\right)^{2}\\
\leq&\;C_{0}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}+Q_{0}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q^{2},\end{split}

where
C_{0}
and
C
are universal constants depending only on
p
.
Here we used the fact that
\lambda=\lambda_{0}/2
.

Similarly, by (
4.12
), Lemma
3.2
, and (
3.9
) in Lemma
3.8
, with
\gamma^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2p}\in(0,\gamma)
, for any
t\in(0,T]
,

(4.20)

\begin{split}&\;(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|v(\cdot,t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\leq&\;(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\Big(\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big)\\
\leq&\;C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-1}t^{-\gamma^{\prime}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma^{\prime}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}+C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\\
&\;+C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\cdot\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\\
\leq&\;C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-1}t^{-\frac{1}{2p}}M_{0}^{2}+C_{1}Q_{0}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q^{2},\end{split}

where
C
and
C_{1}
are universal constants that only depend on
p
.
In the last line, we used (
4.17
), (
4.18
), and the fact
\gamma^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2p}
.
Moreover,

(4.21)

\begin{split}&\;(\nu t)^{\gamma}\|v(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\leq&\;(\nu t)^{\gamma}\Big(\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big)\\
\leq&\;C(\nu t)^{\gamma}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}t^{-\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}+C(\nu t)^{\gamma}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\\
&\;+C(\nu t)^{\gamma}\cdot\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{1/2}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}\\
\leq&\;C\nu^{\gamma}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}M_{0}\big(\rho(T)+T^{\sigma}\big)+C_{2}(\nu t)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q_{0}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q^{2},\end{split}

where
C
and
C_{2}
are universal constants that depend on
p
and
\gamma
.

Regarding the time-continuity of
v
, we derive by Lemma
3.3
, Lemma
3.9
, and Lemma
A.1
that, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
,

(4.22)

\begin{split}&\;|v(x,t_{1})-v(x,t_{2})|\\
\leq&\;\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t_{1})-u_{X}^{\nu}(t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(e^{\nu(t_{2}-t_{1})\Delta}-\mathrm{Id})e^{\nu t_{1}\Delta}u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|B_{\nu}[u](t_{1})-B_{\nu}[u](t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&\;C\big(\nu|t_{1}-t_{2}|\big)^{\gamma/2}t_{1}^{-\gamma}\cdot\lambda^{-1-\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\\
&\;+C(\nu|t_{1}-t_{2}|)^{\gamma/2}(\nu t_{1})^{-\frac{1}{p}-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\\
&\;+C\nu^{-1}\big(\nu(t_{2}-t_{1})\big)^{\gamma/2}(\nu t_{1})^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t_{1}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t_{1}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}\\
&\;+C\nu^{-1}\big(\nu(t_{2}-t_{1})\big)^{\gamma/2}(\nu t_{2})^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t_{2}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t_{2}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}\\
\leq&\;C\big(\nu|t_{1}-t_{2}|\big)^{\gamma/2}(\nu t_{1})^{-\gamma}\left[\nu^{\gamma}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}M_{0}\big(\rho(T)+T^{\sigma}\big)+(\nu t_{1})^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q_{0}+\nu^{-1}(\nu t_{2})^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q^{2}\right],\end{split}

where the constant
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.
Since
p\in(2,\infty)
and
\gamma\geq\frac{2}{p}
, for any
0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
,

(4.23)

\begin{split}&\;(\nu t_{1})^{\gamma}\cdot\frac{|v(x,t_{1})-v(x,t_{2})|}{(\nu|t_{1}-t_{2}|)^{\gamma/2}}\\
\leq&\;C\nu^{\gamma}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}M_{0}\big(\rho(T)+T^{\sigma}\big)+C_{3}(\nu T)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q_{0}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu T)^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q^{2},\end{split}

where
C
and
C_{3}
are universal constants that depend on
p
and
\gamma
.

In view of these bounds, we shall choose from now on

(4.24)

Q:=\begin{cases}C_{0}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}+(C_{1}+1)Q_{0},&\mbox{if }\gamma>\frac{2}{p},\\
C_{0}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}+(C_{1}+C_{2}+C_{3}+1)Q_{0},&\mbox{if }\gamma=\frac{2}{p}.\end{cases}

where
C_{0}
,
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
and
C_{3}
were defined in (
4.19
)-(
4.23
), respectively.
We note that if
\gamma=\frac{2}{p}
,
C_{2}
and
C_{3}
only depend on
p
, so
Q
is always determined by
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
only.
Since
p\in(2,\infty)
and
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
, by combining this choice of
Q
with the estimates above, we may further assume
T\ll 1
to achieve that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|v(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Q,\quad\sup_{t\in(0,T]}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|v(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Q,\quad\sup_{t\in(0,T]}(\nu t)^{\gamma}\|v(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Q,

and

(4.25)

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sup_{0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T}(\nu t_{1})^{\gamma}\cdot\frac{|v(x,t_{1})-v(x,t_{2})|}{(\nu(t_{1}-t_{2}))^{\gamma/2}}\leq Q.

In summary, we can guarantee
v\in\mathcal{U}
by choosing
Q
as in (
4.24
) and assuming
T\ll 1
, where the required smallness of
T
depends on
\nu
,
p
,
\gamma
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
,
Q_{0}
, and
X_{0}
.

Next we turn to estimating
Y
given in (
4.13
).
It suffices to bound
W[u,X;\nu,u_{0}]
defined in (
2.20
); for brevity, we shall write it as
W[u,X]
or even
W
in the sequel.

For that purpose, we first estimate
h_{X}^{\nu}
by incorporating the bounds for
X
with Lemma
3.6
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose
T\leq 1
.
Let
X\in\mathcal{X}
.
Then for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right),

and

(4.26)

\begin{split}\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq&\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left[1+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}\big(\lambda^{-2}\nu t\big)^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\gamma}\right]\\
&\;\cdot\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right),\end{split}

where the constant
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.

As a result,

\displaystyle\|h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left[1+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}\big(\lambda^{-2}\nu t\big)^{\gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\gamma)}\right]

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right),

where the constant
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.

Proof.

We apply (
3.2
) in Lemma
3.6
with the parameters there chosen as follows:

(\gamma^{\prime},\mu_{0},\mu,\mu^{\prime}):=\left(1-\frac{2}{p},\,\frac{1}{p},\,\gamma,\,1-\frac{1}{p}\right).

Then, for
k=0,1
,

\displaystyle\;\|\nabla^{k}h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left[(\nu t)^{-\frac{k}{2}}\min\left(\lambda^{-1},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}(\nu t)^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1-k+2\gamma}\right]M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{k}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\min\left(\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\right)t^{\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\left[1+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}\int_{2\lambda^{2}/(\nu t)}^{1}\zeta^{-\frac{1+k}{2}}\,d\zeta\right]

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\lambda^{-1}\left(M_{0}^{2}N+M_{0}^{2}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\mu^{\prime}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}\right),

where
C
depends on
k
,
p
, and
\gamma
.
By interpolation and the fact
t\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\mu^{\prime}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}=\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma^{\prime}+\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\left(2\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right)^{1-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{\gamma}}\left(t^{\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)^{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{\gamma}}\leq 2N.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left[(\nu t)^{-\frac{k}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}(\nu t)^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1-k+2\gamma}\right]M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{k}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\min\left(\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\right)t^{\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left[1+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}\int_{2\lambda^{2}/(\nu t)}^{1}\zeta^{-\frac{1+k}{2}}\,d\zeta\right].

If
k=0
, with in mind that
\gamma>\frac{1}{p}
, we have

\|h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N.

If
k=1
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left[(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}(\nu t)^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-2+2\gamma}\right]M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\min\big(1,\,\lambda^{2}(\nu t)^{-1}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}t^{\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\ln\left(2+\frac{\nu t}{\lambda^{2}}\right).

Since

\min\big(1,\,\lambda^{2}(\nu t)^{-1}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\ln\left(2+\frac{\nu t}{\lambda^{2}}\right)\leq C\left(1+\frac{\nu t}{\lambda^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\ln\left(2+\frac{\nu t}{\lambda^{2}}\right)\leq C

for some constant
C
that only depends on
p
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left[(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}(\nu t)^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-2+2\gamma}\right]M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left[1+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}\big(\lambda^{-2}\nu t\big)^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\gamma}\right]

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right),

where
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.

Finally, the Hölder estimate follows from the interpolation inequality

\displaystyle\|h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\big(2\|h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big)^{1-\gamma}\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{\gamma}.

∎

Recall that
W[u,X]
was defined in (
2.20
).
By applying Lemma
3.1
, (
3.9
) in Lemma
3.8
, Lemma
3.10
, Lemma
4.2
, and Lemma
A.1
, we derive with
\gamma^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{p}\in(0,\gamma)
that, for
t\in(0,T]
,

(4.27)

\begin{split}&\;\|W[u,X](t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;\|g_{X}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\big\|h_{X}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X(t)})+B_{\nu}[u](t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\leq&\;C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma^{\prime}}}+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)\\
&\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{0}-u_{X(t)}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\\
&\;+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\cdot\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\\
\leq&\;Ct^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{3}+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)\\
&\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q^{2},\end{split}

where the constant
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.
Combining this with (
4.13
) and Lemma
A.2
, we find that

(4.28)

\begin{split}\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq&\;\int_{0}^{t}\|W[u,X](\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\,d\tau\\
\leq&\;Ct^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\left[\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)\right.\\
&\;\left.\qquad\quad+\nu^{-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q^{2}\right],\end{split}

where
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.

Take
\beta\in(0,1)
to be chosen later.
Recall that
\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
, so
0<\frac{\beta}{2}<\gamma
.
By Lemma
3.10
, the interpolation inequality, and the definition of
\mathcal{X}
,

(4.29)

\begin{split}\|g_{X}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq&\;C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta/2}_{s}}^{2}\\
\leq&\;C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{3-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}^{\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}\leq Ct^{-\beta}\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{3-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}\\
\leq&\;Ct^{-\beta}\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{3-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}\big(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\big)^{\frac{\beta}{\gamma}},\end{split}

where the constant
C
depends on
\beta
and
\gamma
.

By Lemma
A.1
and Lemma
3.1
,

(4.30)

\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X(t)})\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(\nu t)^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{0}-u_{X(t)}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\leq C(\nu t)^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right),

where
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
, and similarly,

\big\|\nabla e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X(t)})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right),

where
C
only depends on
p
.

Finally, by (
3.11
) in Lemma
3.8
,

(4.31)

\begin{split}\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq&\;C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}\\
\leq&\;C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q^{2},\end{split}

where
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.
Now by (
4.13
) and Lemma
A.4
, with
\beta:=\frac{1+\gamma}{2}\in(\gamma,1)
, it holds for any
t\in[0,T]
that

(4.32)

\begin{split}&\;\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\beta}\|g_{X}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\big[h_{X}^{\nu}+e^{\nu\eta\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X(\eta)})\big]\circ X(\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|B_{\nu}[u]\circ X(\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}.\end{split}

We remark that we treated different terms on the right-hand side differently not only to help close the bound in (
4.35
) below, but also to achieve the optimal
\nu
-dependence of the estimate in the last two terms, which will be useful in Section
6
.
Combining this with (
4.28
) and all the estimates and also applying Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
B.1
, we will find that

(4.33)

\begin{split}&\;\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{C^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\beta}\|g_{X}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(\eta)+\nabla e^{\nu\eta\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X(\eta)})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{T})}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{s}}\\
&\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|B_{\nu}[u](\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{s}}^{\gamma}\\
\leq&\;Ct^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\left[\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)\right.\\
&\;\left.\qquad\quad+\nu^{-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q^{2}\right]\\
&\;+C\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{3-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}\big(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\big)^{\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)M_{0}\\
&\;+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left[1+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}\big(\lambda^{-2}\nu t\big)^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\gamma}\right]\\
&\;\quad\cdot\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)M_{0}\\
&\;+Ct^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}\nu^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q^{2}M_{0}^{\gamma}.\end{split}

where
C
is a constant depending on
p
and
\gamma
.
Since
\gamma>1-\frac{2}{p}
,
\gamma\geq\frac{2}{p}
, and
t\leq 1
, in short we have

(4.34)

\begin{split}&\;\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{C^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)\left[\left(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\right)^{\beta/\gamma}+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\right].\end{split}

Recall that the notation
C=C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)
represents a universal constant that only depends on
\gamma
,
p
,
\nu
,
\lambda_{0}
,
Q_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
N
.
Here we used the facts that
\lambda=\lambda_{0}/2
and that
Q
depends only on
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
.
Thus it follows that, for any
t\in(0,T]
,

(4.35)

\begin{split}t^{\gamma}\|Y(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq&\;t^{\gamma}\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)\left[\left(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\right)^{\beta/\gamma}+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\right]+\rho(t).\end{split}

Since
\beta>\gamma
and
\sigma\in(0,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p})
,
we may take
T
sufficiently small, which depends on
\gamma
,
p
,
\nu
,
\lambda_{0}
,
Q_{0}
,
M_{0}
,
N
, and
X_{0}
, so that for all
t\in(0,T]
,

(4.36)

\|Y(\cdot,t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\|_{C^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|Y(\cdot,t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\big(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\big).

For future use, we point out that, with this choice of
T
as well as (
4.14
),
Y
also satisfies (
4.17
) and (
4.18
).

To bound
\partial_{t}Y
, we first justify

(4.37)

\partial_{t}Y=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda Y+W[u,X].

Observe from (
2.10
), (
2.13
), (
2.20
), and (
4.12
) that

(4.38)

W[u,X](t)=v\big(X(t),t\big)+\frac{1}{4}\Lambda X(t).

By the regularity of
v
shown in (
4.21
), the time-continuity of
v
shown in (
4.25
), and the time-continuity of
X\in\mathcal{X}
noted in Remark
4.2
, we can show that
t\mapsto W[u,X](t)
is continuous in the
C(\mathbb{T})
-topology on
(0,T)
.
From (
4.29
)-(
4.31
) and Lemma
4.2
, we can derive a local
C^{\beta}
-bound for
W[u,X]
with
\beta\in[\frac{2}{p},\gamma]
as follows (cf. (
4.33
)):

(4.39)

\begin{split}&\;\|W[u,X](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;Ct^{-\beta}\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{3}\\
&\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left[1+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}\big(\lambda^{-2}\nu t\big)^{\beta(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\gamma)}\right]\\
&\;\quad\cdot\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)M_{0}^{\beta}\\
&\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)M_{0}^{\beta}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q^{2}M_{0}^{\beta}.\end{split}

Since
\beta\geq\frac{2}{p}
, Lemma
A.4
gives the local
C^{1,\beta}
-bound for
\mathcal{I}[W]
on
(0,T)
:

(4.40)

\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\beta}\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\beta}\|W[u,X](\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}.

Hence, by Lemma
A.4
,
\partial_{t}Y(s,t)
is well-defined pointwise for any
t\in(0,T)
, and (
4.37
) holds.

Now by (
4.37
), the interpolation inequalities, and the fact
T\leq 1
,

(4.41)

\begin{split}&\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}Y(\cdot,\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}Y(\cdot,\eta)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\Lambda Y(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|W[u,X](\eta)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|\Lambda Y(\eta)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\eta^{\gamma}\big\|W[u,X](\eta)\big\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|Y^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{1/p}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|Y^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|W[u,X](\eta)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|W[u,X](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},\end{split}

where
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.
We use this, (
4.27
), (
4.33
), (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) (with
\beta:=\gamma
there), as well as the interpolation inequalities to find that, for
t\in(0,T]
with
T\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}Y(\cdot,\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}Y(\cdot,t)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|W[u,X](\eta)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|W[u,X](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{3}+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot T^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}T^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q^{2}

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}T^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left[1+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu T\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}\big(\lambda^{-2}\nu T\big)^{\gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\gamma)}\right]

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N\cdot T^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)M_{0}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}T^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)M_{0}^{\gamma}+C\nu^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}T^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q^{2}M_{0}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0})\left(1+N^{\gamma}+T^{\frac{1}{p}}N\right).

By taking
T
to be sufficiently small, which depends on
\gamma
,
p
,
\nu
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
, and also using Young’s inequality, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}Y(\cdot,\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}Y(\cdot,t)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}N+C_{*}(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0}).

Then we choose

(4.42)

N:=2C_{*}(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0}),

which implies

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}Y(\cdot,\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}Y(\cdot,t)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq N.

We remind that these quantitative estimates for
Y
are enough to justify as in Remark
4.2
that
Y\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\mathbb{T}))
.

Finally, let us show that
Y\in C^{1}_{loc}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T})
.
Since
t\mapsto\|\partial_{t}Y(t)\|_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}
and
t\mapsto\|Y^{\prime}(t)\|_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}
are locally bounded in
(0,T)
,
t\mapsto Y(t)
is locally continuous in the
C^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{T})
-topology for any
\beta\in[0,\gamma)
on
(0,T)
by interpolation;
see (
3.42
), (
3.43
), and Remark
4.2
.
Hence,
t\mapsto\Lambda Y
is locally continuous in the
C(\mathbb{T})
-topology on
(0,T)
.
By (
4.37
) and the continuity of
t\mapsto W[u,X]
obtained from (
4.38
), this further implies that
\partial_{t}Y\in C_{loc}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T})
.
This together with the fact that
Y^{\prime}\in C([0,T];C(\mathbb{T}))
gives that
Y\in C^{1}_{loc}((0,T)\times\mathbb{T})
.

Combining all the above characterizations of
Y
, we obtain that
Y\in\mathcal{X}
.

In summary, by defining
Q
and
N
as in (
4.24
) and (
4.42
) respectively, and then taking
T
to be sufficiently small, which depends on
\gamma,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda_{0},\,Q_{0},\,M_{0}
and
X_{0}
, we can guarantee that, whenever
(u,X)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
, then
(v,Y):=\mathcal{T}(u,X)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
.

4.3.
Contraction mapping and the proof of Proposition
4.1

Next we show that, by taking
T
to be smaller if necessary, we can make
\mathcal{T}
a contraction mapping from
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
to itself under the metric (
4.11
).

Take
(u_{i},X_{i})\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
(
i=1,2
), and let
(v_{i},Y_{i}):=\mathcal{T}(u_{i},X_{i})
.
It is known that
X_{i}\in O^{\lambda}_{T}
with
\lambda
defined above, and they satisfy (
4.17
) and (
4.18
).
Moreover, by the definition of
\mathcal{X}
and (
4.14
),

\|X_{1}^{\prime}-X_{2}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\|X_{1}^{\prime}-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{8}<\frac{\lambda}{2},

and by (
4.15
), for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]
,

\|X_{i}^{\prime}(t_{1})-X_{i}^{\prime}(t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\|X_{i}^{\prime}(t_{1})-X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\|X_{i}^{\prime}(t_{2})-X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{4}=\frac{\lambda}{2}.

Since
X_{1}(s,0)=X_{2}(s,0)
, by (
4.12
), Lemma
3.5
(with
\mu_{0}=\frac{1}{p}
there) and (
3.13
) in Lemma
3.8
, for any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\;\|v_{1}(t)-v_{2}(t)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\|u_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)-u_{X_{2}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|B_{\nu}[u_{1}](t)-B_{\nu}[u_{2}](t)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-1-\gamma}M_{0}^{2}\Big[t^{1-\gamma-\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}(X_{1}-X_{2})(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X_{1}^{\prime}-X_{2}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{s}}\Big]

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\nu^{-1}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|(u_{1}-u_{2})(\eta)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u_{1}(\eta),u_{2}(\eta))\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}},

where the constant
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.
Using the bounds for
(u_{i},X_{i})
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\;(\nu t)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\|v_{1}(t)-v_{2}(t)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}M_{0}^{2}\Big[t^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}(X_{1}-X_{2})(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+t^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\|X_{1}^{\prime}-X_{2}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{s}}\Big]

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\|(u_{1}-u_{2})(\eta)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}}.

This, together with the facts that
p\in(2,\infty)
and
\gamma\in(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
, implies that

(4.43)

\begin{split}&\;d_{\mathcal{U}}(v_{1},v_{2})\\
\leq&\;C\big(T^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}+T^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\nu^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}M_{0}^{2}\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})+CT^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q\cdot d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2}),\end{split}

where
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.

Next we bound
d_{\mathcal{X}}(Y_{1},Y_{2})
.
By (
4.37
), we argue as in (
4.40
) and (
4.41
) that

(4.44)

\begin{split}d_{\mathcal{X}}(Y_{1},Y_{2})\leq&\;\|Y_{1}^{\prime}-Y_{2}^{\prime}\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|(Y_{1}^{\prime}-Y_{2}^{\prime})(\cdot,\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},\end{split}

where the constant
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.

By Lemma
A.1
and Lemma
B.1
, we derive from (
2.20
) that

(4.45)

\begin{split}&\;\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](t)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;\|g_{X_{1}}(t)-g_{X_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\|H_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)-H_{X_{2}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+\big\|\big[e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})+B_{\nu}[u_{1}]\big]\circ X_{1}(t)-\big[e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})+B_{\nu}[u_{1}]\big]\circ X_{2}(t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+\big\|\big[e^{\nu t\Delta}(-u_{X_{1}(t)}+u_{X_{2}(t)})+B_{\nu}[u_{1}]-B_{\nu}[u_{2}]\big]\circ X_{2}(t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;\|g_{X_{1}}(t)-g_{X_{2}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\|H_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)-H_{X_{2}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+\Big(\big\|\nabla e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\nabla B_{\nu}[u_{1}](t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big)\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{X_{1}(t)}-u_{X_{2}(t)})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|B_{\nu}[u_{1}](t)-B_{\nu}[u_{2}](t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.\end{split}

In order to bound
H_{X_{1}}^{\nu}-H_{X_{2}}^{\nu}
, we shall apply Lemma
3.7
with
(\gamma,\beta,\mu_{0})
there taken to be
(\frac{\gamma}{2},\gamma,\frac{1}{p})
, and then
(M,N_{0},N_{\beta})
there can be replaced by
(CM_{0},N,N)
due to the assumptions on
X_{i}\in\mathcal{X}
, (
4.17
), (
4.18
), and the interpolation inequalities.
Then we will find with
T\leq 1
that

(4.46)

\begin{split}&\;\|H_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)-H_{X_{2}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+(\nu t)^{\gamma/2}M_{0}^{-\gamma}\|H_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)-H_{X_{2}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;Cd_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\\
&\;\cdot\Big[\lambda^{-3}M_{0}^{3-\gamma/2}N^{\gamma/2}\big(1+\nu^{-1}T^{1-\frac{2}{p}}N^{2}\big)+\lambda^{-1}M_{0}\\
&\;\quad+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-2-\gamma/2}\nu^{-(1-\gamma/2)/2}M_{0}^{2}N\big(1+\nu^{-1}T^{1-\frac{2}{p}}N^{2}\big)^{\frac{2+\gamma}{4}}\\
&\;\quad+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-1-\gamma/2}\nu^{-(1-\gamma/2)/2}M_{0}^{2}\Big]\\
\leq&\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M_{0},N)\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\Big(1+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{p}}\Big).\end{split}

Applying Lemma
3.1
, Lemma
3.4
(with
(\beta,\gamma)
there taken to be
(\frac{1}{p},\gamma)
), (
3.12
) and (
3.13
) in Lemma
3.8
, Lemma
3.11
(with
\gamma=\frac{1}{2p}
there), we derive from (
4.45
) that, for
t\in(0,T]
with
T\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](t)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{2}\Big[\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2p}}}+\lambda^{-1}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2p}}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2p}}}\big)\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\Big]

\displaystyle\;+C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M_{0},N)\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\Big(1+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{p}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+C\Big[(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\;\qquad+(\nu t)^{-1}\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q\big((\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q+(\nu t)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}Q\big)\Big]\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{1}{p}}M_{0}\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}\Big(t^{-\gamma}M_{0}^{2}\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+M_{0}^{2}\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q\cdot\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\gamma/2}\|(u_{1}-u_{2})(\eta)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}M_{0}^{2}\Big[t^{-\frac{1}{2p}}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})+\lambda^{-1}\cdot t^{-\frac{1}{2p}}M_{0}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\Big]

\displaystyle\;+C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M_{0},N)\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\Big(1+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{p}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\Big[Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}+\nu^{-1}Q^{2}\big((\nu T)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}+(\nu T)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\big)\Big]\cdot t^{1-\frac{1}{p}}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{1}{p}}M_{0}\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}\Big(t^{-\gamma}M_{0}^{2}\cdot t^{1-\frac{1}{p}}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})+M_{0}^{2}\cdot t^{1-\gamma}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}Q\cdot(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\|(u_{1}-u_{2})(\eta)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}},

where the constant
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.
In the last inequality, we used the following estimates due to the fact
X_{1}(s,0)=X_{2}(s,0)
:

(4.47)

\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}(X_{1}-X_{2})(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\,d\eta\leq Ct^{1-\frac{1}{p}}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2}),

and similarly,

(4.48)

\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\leq Ct^{1-\gamma}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2}).

Hence, under the assumptions
0<t<T\leq 1
and
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](t)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\Big(1+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{p}}+t^{-\frac{1}{2p}}+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}+t^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q\cdot d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2})t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\Big(t^{-\frac{1}{2p}}+t^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\Big)+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q\cdot d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2})t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}},

which gives

(4.49)

\begin{split}&\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)\big[d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})+d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2})\big]\Big(T^{\frac{1}{2p}}+T^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\Big).\end{split}

Similarly,

\displaystyle\;\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](t)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\|g_{X_{1}}(t)-g_{X_{2}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+\|H_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)-H_{X_{2}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\;+\big\|\big[e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})\big]\circ X_{1}(t)-\big[e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{2}(t)})\big]\circ X_{2}(t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\;+\|B_{\nu}[u_{1}]\circ X_{1}(t)-B_{\nu}[u_{1}]\circ X_{2}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+\|(B_{\nu}[u_{1}]-B_{\nu}[u_{2}])\circ X_{2}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}.

By Lemma
3.11
,

\displaystyle\;\|g_{X_{1}}(t)-g_{X_{2}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}M_{0}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\cdot\Big[\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\lambda^{-1}\big(\|X^{\prime}_{1}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}M_{0}\cdot t^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|(X_{1}^{\prime},X_{2}^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{s}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left[\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\big)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\;\cdot\Big[t^{-\gamma/2}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma/2}\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)-X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\lambda^{-1}t^{-\gamma/2}M_{0}\|X_{1}^{\prime}-X_{2}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-3}M_{0}^{5/2}\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})t^{-\gamma}\big(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
C
depends on
\gamma
.
In the last line, we used the bound for
t^{\gamma}\|X_{i}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}
in the definition of
\mathcal{X}
.
The Hölder estimate for
(H_{X_{1}}^{\nu}-H_{X_{2}}^{\nu})
in (
4.46
) reads

\|H_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)-H_{X_{2}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M_{0},N)\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\Big(1+t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{1}{p}}\Big)t^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}.

By Lemma
3.1
, Lemma
3.4
, Lemma
A.1
, Lemma
B.1
, (
4.47
), and (
4.48
), for any
t\in(0,T]
with
T\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\;\left\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})\circ X_{1}(t)-e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{2}(t)})\circ X_{2}(t)\right\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\left\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})\circ X_{1}(t)-e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})\circ X_{2}(t)\right\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\;+\left\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{X_{1}(t)}-u_{X_{2}(t)})\circ X_{2}(t)\right\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1}}\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}

\displaystyle\;+C\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{1}(t)})\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1,\gamma}}\|(X_{1}^{\prime}(t),X_{2}^{\prime}(t))\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{\gamma}\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}

\displaystyle\;+\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{X_{1}(t)}-u_{X_{2}(t)})\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}+\|u_{X_{1}(t)}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\right)\cdot t^{1-\gamma}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}+\|u_{X_{1}(t)}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\right)\cdot M_{0}^{\gamma}\cdot t^{1-\frac{1}{p}}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle\;+C\Big[(\nu t)^{-\gamma/2}\lambda^{-1}M_{0}\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\;\qquad+(\nu t)^{-1/2}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}\cdot M_{0}^{2}\big(t^{-\gamma}\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\big)\Big]M_{0}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\big(Q_{0}+\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\big)\Big[(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot t^{1-\gamma}+(\nu t)^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot M_{0}^{\gamma}\cdot t^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\Big]d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle\;+C\Big[(\nu t)^{-\gamma/2}\lambda^{-1}M_{0}+(\nu t)^{-1/2}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}\cdot M_{0}^{2}\cdot t^{1-\gamma-\frac{1}{p}}\Big]M_{0}^{\gamma}\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,Q_{0},M_{0})\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}-\gamma}.

By (
3.12
) in Lemma
3.8
, Lemma
A.1
and Lemma
B.1
, for any
t\in(0,T]
with
T\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|B_{\nu}[u_{1}]\circ X_{1}(t)-B_{\nu}[u_{1}]\circ X_{2}(t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\big\|B_{\nu}[u_{1}](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1}}\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}+C\big\|B_{\nu}[u_{1}](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1,\gamma}}\|(X_{1}^{\prime},X_{2}^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}}^{\gamma}\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-1}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{1/2}\|u_{1}(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\left[\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{1}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{(1+\gamma)/2}\|u_{1}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}}\right]

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left[(\nu t)^{-1}\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}+(\nu t)^{-(2+\gamma)/2}\cdot M_{0}^{\gamma}\cdot\|X_{1}(t)-X_{2}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q\left[(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q+(\nu t)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}Q\right]

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left[(\nu t)^{-1}\cdot t^{1-\gamma}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})+(\nu t)^{-\frac{2+\gamma}{2}}\cdot M_{0}^{\gamma}\cdot t^{1-\frac{1}{p}}d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0})\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})t^{1-\frac{1}{p}-\frac{3\gamma}{2}}.

Finally, by (
3.14
) in Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
B.1
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|\big(B_{\nu}[u_{1}]-B_{\nu}[u_{2}]\big)\circ X_{2}(t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\big\|B_{\nu}[u_{1}](t)-B_{\nu}[u_{2}](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|X_{2}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\gamma/2}\|(u_{1}-u_{2})(\eta)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{1/2}\|(u_{1}(\eta),u_{2}(\eta))\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\cdot M_{0}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\cdot(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2})\cdot(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q\cdot M_{0}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}QM_{0}^{\gamma}\cdot d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2})t^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}.

Summarizing all the estimates and using the assumption
T\leq 1
yields that

(4.50)

\begin{split}&\;\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](t)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\lambda^{-3}M_{0}^{5/2}\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})t^{-\gamma}\big(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)\cdot d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}-\gamma}\\
&\;+C\nu^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}QM_{0}^{\gamma}\cdot d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2})t^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}},\end{split}

which gives, under the assumptions
T\leq 1
and
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
,

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)\big[d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})+d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2})\big]\left[\big(\rho(T)+T^{\sigma}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\right].

Putting this and (
4.49
) into (
4.44
), and also using (
4.43
), and the definitions of
Q
and
N
in (
4.24
) and (
4.42
) respectively, we obtain that

(4.51)

\begin{split}&\;d_{\mathcal{U}}(v_{1},v_{2})+d_{\mathcal{X}}(Y_{1},Y_{2})\\
\leq&\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{0},Q_{0},M_{0},N)\left(T^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}+T^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}+T^{\frac{1}{2p}}+\big(\rho(T)+T^{\sigma}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\\
&\;\cdot\big[d_{\mathcal{U}}(u_{1},u_{2})+d_{\mathcal{X}}(X_{1},X_{2})\big].\end{split}

Since
\gamma\in(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
,
\sigma>0
, and
\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\rho(t)=0
, we may take
T
smaller if necessary, such that for all
u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathcal{U}
,
X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathcal{X}
, and
(v_{i},Y_{i}):=\mathcal{T}(u_{i},X_{i})

(i=1,2)
, we have
(v_{i},Y_{i})\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
, and

d_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}}((v_{1},Y_{1}),(v_{2},Y_{2}))\leq\frac{1}{2}d_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}}((u_{1},X_{1}),(u_{2},X_{2})),

so
\mathcal{T}
is a contraction mapping from
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
to itself.

Therefore,
\mathcal{T}
admits a unique fixed-point in
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{X}
, which we denote by
(u,X)
with slight abuse of the notations.
It is clearly a mild local solution on
[0,T]
to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) in the sense of Definition
2.1
, where
T\leq 1
essentially depends on
\gamma,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda_{0},\,Q_{0},\,M_{0}
, and
X_{0}
.
We remark that the implicit dependence of
T
on
X_{0}
stems from the requirement that
(\rho(T)+T^{\sigma})
and
\phi(T)
should be smaller than some constants that only depend on
\gamma,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda_{0},\,Q_{0}
, and
M_{0}
.

Finally, in the statement of Proposition
4.1
, (
4.8
) follows from (
4.37
) and the fact that
(u,X)
is a fixed-point of
\mathcal{T}
, while the other bounds for
X
in the statement have been established in (
4.16
)-(
4.18
).

5.
Properties of the Mild Solutions

Throughout this section, we let
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
and
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, which satisfy
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
Suppose for some
T>0
,
(u,X)
is a mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
2.1
;
note that it is not necessarily obtained from the construction in Section
4
.

Our goal in this section is to establish several fundamental properties of
(u,X)
.
We will show that the mild solution is unique (Proposition
5.1
) and enjoys estimates in various regularity class (see Lemma
5.1
, Lemma
5.2
, Lemma
5.3
, and Proposition
5.4
).
Continuation of the mild solution will be studied in Proposition
5.2
.
We will show in Corollary
5.3
that there exists a unique maximal mild solution, and in the case a finite-time singularity occurs (see Definition
2.2
), we provide characterization of the solution when it approaches the blow-up time.

5.1.
Regularity of the mild solutions

Throughout the section, with abuse of the notations, we denote that

(5.1)

M:=\sup_{\eta\in[0,T]}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{1/p}_{s}}<+\infty,

(5.2)

\lambda:=\inf_{\eta\in[0,T]}|X(\eta)|_{*}>0,

and

(5.3)

Q:=\sup_{\eta\in[0,T]}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}<+\infty.

These quantities are well-defined thanks to the definition of the mild solutions (see Definition
2.1
).

Lemma 5.1
.

(5.4)

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

Proof.

Recall that
u
satisfies the representation (
2.18
).

On one hand, by Lemma
3.2
and Lemma
A.1
, for
t\in(0,T]
,

\big\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\big\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}+\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\leq C\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M^{2}+Q,

where
C
depends on
p
, and

\big\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Ct^{-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/p}}+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{x}},

which gives

(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\nu^{\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1}M^{2}+CQ,

where
C
depends on
p
. By interpolation, for any
q\in[p,\infty]
and any
t\in(0,T]
,

(5.5)

(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}}\big\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\big\|_{L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

On the other hand, by (
3.6
) in Lemma
3.8
, for any
q,r\in[p,\infty]
satisfying
\frac{1}{q}\in(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{p}]
,

(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}}\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{1-\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}}\|(u\otimes u)(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{pr}{p+r}}},

which together with (
2.18
) and (
5.5
) implies

(5.6)

(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}}\|u(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq C(p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{r}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{r}}.

Here
C
depends on
p
,
q
, and
r
.
If
p>4
, we can simply take
(r,q)=(p,\infty)
and that gives (
5.4
).
Otherwise, we first take
r=r_{0}:=p
and choose
q=q_{1}
such that
\frac{1}{q_{1}}=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p})\in(\frac{2}{p}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{p}]
, which leads to

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

Then we bootstrap with (
5.6
), each time by taking
r_{k}=q_{k-1}
and
\frac{1}{q_{k}}=\max(0,\frac{1}{r_{k}}-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}))
there.
After finite steps of iteration, we end up having (
5.4
).
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

u\in C([0,T];L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
.

Proof.

Recall that
p\in(2,\infty)
and
u
satisfies (
2.18
).

Since
u_{0}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
t\mapsto e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}
is continuous in
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

The time-continuity in
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
of
u_{X}^{\nu}
at
t=0
follows from Lemma
3.2
, while the time-continuity at some
t\in(0,T]
can be justified by interpolating the estimate in Lemma
3.3
and, say, an
L^{2}
-estimate of
u_{X}^{\nu}(t)
due to Lemma
3.2
:

\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{4}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}.

Regarding
B_{\nu}[u]
, its time-continuity in
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
at
t=0
follows from (
3.8
) in Lemma
3.8
:

\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\leq C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\right)^{2}\leq C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{p}}^{2}.

Its time-continuity at some
t\in(0,T]
follows by interpolating the estimate in Lemma
3.9
and, say, an
L^{p/2}
-estimate for
B_{\nu}[u]
from (
3.6
) in Lemma
3.8
:

\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{L^{p/2}_{x}}\leq C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{2}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\|(u\otimes u)(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p/2}}\leq C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{p}}^{2}.

Combining them, we conclude that
u\in C([0,T];L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

For any
t\in(0,T)
,
\partial_{t}X(s,t)
is well-defined pointwise, and the equation

(5.7)

\partial_{t}X(s,t)=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda X(s,t)+W[u,X;\nu,u_{0}](s,t)=u(X(s,t),t)

holds.
As a result,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

Proof.

Proceeding as in (
4.21
), we obtain that, for any
t\in(0,T]
,

(5.8)

\begin{split}&\;\|u(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1/p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\leq&\;\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1/p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1/p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1/p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\leq&\;C\lambda^{-1-\frac{1}{p}}t^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/p}}+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{3}{2p}}\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\\
&\;+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-(1+\frac{1}{p})/2}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{1/2}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}\\
\leq&\;C\lambda^{-1-\frac{1}{p}}t^{-\frac{1}{p}}M^{2}+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{3}{2p}}Q+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{5}{2p}}\left(Q+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}.\end{split}

Thanks to Lemma
5.1
and the fact
p>2
,

(5.9)

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{3}{2p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1/p}}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)<+\infty.

Interpolating this with the
L^{p}
-time-continuity of
u
due to Lemma
5.2
, we know that, for any
\beta\in[0,\frac{1}{p})
,
t\mapsto u(x,t)
is continuous in
(0,T]
in the
C^{\beta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
-topology.

The rest of the argument is similar to that right after (
4.38
).
From the
C^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
-time-continuity of
t\mapsto X(s,t)
as well as the local
C^{1/p}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
-bound of
X(s,t)
, one can deduce that
t\mapsto X(t)
is continuous in
(0,T]
in the
C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
-topology for any
\beta\in[0,\frac{1}{p})
.
Observe that (cf. (
4.38
))

W[u,X]=u\circ X+\frac{1}{4}\Lambda X,

so (
5.9
), Lemma
5.1
, and the bound for
X
imply that

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|W[u,X](\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}<+\infty,\quad\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{3}{2p}}\|W[u,X](\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/p}}<+\infty,

Since
p>2
, by Lemma
A.4
,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\big\|\mathcal{I}[W](\eta)\big\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}<+\infty,\quad\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{3}{2p}}\big\|\mathcal{I}[W](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\frac{1}{p}}}<+\infty.

Moreover, the regularity and time-continuity of
u
and
X
implies that
t\mapsto W[u,X]
is continuous in the
C_{s}(\mathbb{T})
-topology in
(0,T]
.
Hence, by Lemma
A.4
again,
\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}[W](t)
is well-defined pointwise for all
t\in(0,T)
, and

\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}[W](t)=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t)+W[u,X](t).

Then the desired claims follow from the representation (
2.19
) of
X
.

Lastly, the
L^{\infty}
-bound for
\partial_{t}X
follows from Lemma
5.1
and Lemma
B.1
:

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

∎

Remark 5.1
.

For future use, we remark that, by Lemma
B.1
and (
5.9
), one can similarly show that

(5.10)

\begin{split}\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{3}{2p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{1/p}(\mathbb{T})}\leq&\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{3}{2p}}\left(\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|u(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1/p}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{1/p}\right)\\
\leq&\;C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).\end{split}

In the next lemma, we shall prove that the mild solution
(u,X)
admits improved regularity than what is dictated in Definition
2.1
.

Lemma 5.4
.

For any
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
,

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|u(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

Proof.

We first bound a good part of the representation (
2.19
).
Thanks to (
3.11
) in Lemma
3.8
and Lemma
5.1
, for any
\beta\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
, and any
t\in(0,T]
,

(5.11)

\begin{split}&\;\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}+B_{\nu}[u](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\leq&\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}-\frac{\beta}{2}}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}\left(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\tau\in(0,T]}(\nu\tau)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}\\
\leq&\;C(T,\beta,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)\left(t^{-\frac{1}{p}-\frac{\beta}{2}}+t^{\frac{1-\beta}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}\right).\end{split}

Hence, by Lemma
A.4
and Lemma
B.1
,

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\left\|\mathcal{I}\Big[\big(e^{\nu\eta\Delta}u_{0}+B_{\nu}[u]\big)\circ X(\eta)\Big]\right\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\Big\|\big(e^{\nu\eta\Delta}u_{0}+B_{\nu}[u]\big)\circ X(\eta)\Big\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\big\|e^{\nu\eta\Delta}u_{0}+B_{\nu}[u](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\beta}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\beta,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

Next we handle the other terms in (
2.19
).
By Lemma
3.10
, for any
\beta\in(0,1)
,

\big\|g_{X}(t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta/2}}^{2}.

By (
3.3
) in Lemma
3.6
, with the parameter there chosen as

(\alpha,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\mu_{0},\mu,\mu^{\prime}):=\left(\beta,\,\frac{\beta}{2},\,\frac{\beta}{2},\,\frac{1}{p},\,\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{1}{p},\,\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{1}{p}\right),

we derive that

\displaystyle\;\|H_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\lambda^{-1}t^{-\frac{\beta}{2p}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\beta/2}}\right)^{1-\frac{\beta}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta/2}}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\cdot M^{\beta}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{4}}\min\left(\lambda^{-\frac{\beta}{2}},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{\beta}{4}}\right)\lambda^{-1}

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left(t^{1-\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\beta/2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right.

\displaystyle\;\qquad\left.+t^{1-\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta/2}}\right)\cdot M^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{-\frac{\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta}{2p}}\nu^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}\lambda^{-1}M^{1+\beta}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\beta/2}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta/2}}\right)

\displaystyle\;+C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)\cdot t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}-\frac{3\beta}{4}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{4}}\lambda^{-1-\frac{\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\beta/2}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{\beta}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta/2}}\right)\cdot M^{\beta},

where
C
depends on
\beta
and
p
.
By Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
A.1
, for any
\beta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{X(t)}\circ X(t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{X(t)}\big\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\beta}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{\beta}{4}}\|u_{X(t)}\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\beta/2}}M^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{-\frac{3\beta}{4}}\nu^{-\frac{\beta}{4}}\cdot\lambda^{-1-\frac{\beta}{2}}M^{1+\beta}\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta/2}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta/2}_{s}}.

Now we take
\beta=\frac{2}{p}
.
Then Lemma
A.4
and the estimates above (also recall (
5.10
)) give that

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{2}{p}}\Big\|\mathcal{I}[g_{X}+H_{X}^{\nu}-e^{\nu\eta\Delta}u_{X(\eta)}\circ X(\eta)\big]\Big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,2/p}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{2}{p}}\big\|g_{X}(\eta)+H_{X}^{\nu}(\eta)-e^{\nu\eta\Delta}u_{X(\eta)}\circ X(\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{2/p}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{2}{p}}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{2/p}_{s}}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{2}{p}}\left\|\mathcal{I}\Big[\big(e^{\nu\eta\Delta}u_{0}+B_{\nu}[u]\big)\circ X(\eta)\Big]\right\|_{\dot{C}^{1,2/p}_{s}}

\displaystyle\;+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{2}{p}}\Big\|\mathcal{I}[g_{X}+H_{X}^{\nu}-e^{\nu\eta\Delta}u_{X(\eta)}\circ X(\eta)\big]\Big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,2/p}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

By Lemma
5.3
, (
5.11
), as well as the argument in (
5.8
) and (
5.9
), we can further show that

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{2}{p}}\|u\|_{\dot{C}^{2/p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q),

and thus

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{2}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{2/p}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

We may bootstrap with this improved regularity.
For instance, now we can take arbitrary
\beta\in(\frac{2}{p},\frac{4}{p})\cap(0,1)
and apply the preceding estimates once again.
For any fixed
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
, it suffices to repeat the above argument for finitely many times to reach the desired conclusion.
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

For any
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
, and any
0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
,

t_{1}^{\gamma}\|u(t_{1})-u(t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\gamma/2}.

Proof.

One may argue as in (
4.22
), and then apply (
5.1
)-(
5.3
), Lemma
5.1
and Lemma
5.4
to find that, for any
0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
,

\displaystyle\;\|u(t_{1})-u(t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\big(\nu|t_{1}-t_{2}|\big)^{\gamma/2}t_{1}^{-\gamma}\cdot\lambda^{-1-\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu|t_{1}-t_{2}|)^{\gamma/2}(\nu t_{1})^{-\frac{1}{p}-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-1}\big(\nu(t_{2}-t_{1})\big)^{\gamma/2}(\nu t_{1})^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t_{1}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t_{1}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-1}\big(\nu(t_{2}-t_{1})\big)^{\gamma/2}(\nu t_{2})^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t_{2}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t_{2}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\gamma/2}t_{1}^{-\gamma}.

∎

Lemma 5.6
.

For any
\beta\in(0,1)
,

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}t^{\beta}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}=0
.

Proof.

Take an arbitrary
\gamma\in(\beta,1)
.
By the time-continuity of
X
and Lemma
5.4
,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}<+\infty,\quad\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\|X^{\prime}(t)-X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}=0.

On the other hand,
X_{0}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
implies (see (
4.4
))

\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\|X_{0}^{\prime}-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}=0,\quad\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}t^{\beta}\|e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}}=0.

Hence,

\displaystyle\;t^{\beta}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;t^{\beta}\|e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}}+t^{\beta}\|X^{\prime}(t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;t^{\beta}\|e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}}+C\left(t^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}+t^{\gamma}\|e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}\|X^{\prime}(t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq
