\displaystyle\;t^{\beta}\|e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{s}}+C\left(t^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}+\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\gamma}}\left(\|X^{\prime}(t)-X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X_{0}^{\prime}-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right)^{1-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}},

which converges to
0
as
t\to 0^{+}
.
∎

5.2.
Uniqueness and continuation of the mild solutions

Now we prove the uniqueness of the mild solutions.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
and
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, which satisfy
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
Suppose that, for some
T>0
,
(u_{1},X_{1})
and
(u_{2},X_{2})
are two mild solutions to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
2.1
.
Then
u_{1}(t)\equiv u_{2}(t)
and
X_{1}(t)\equiv X_{2}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
.

Remark 5.2
.

As a corollary, the solution constructed in the preceding subsections does not depend on the choice of
\gamma
, and it is the unique mild solution.

Proof.

Fix
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)\cap(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
.
By virtue of Lemma
5.1
, Lemma
5.3
and Lemma
5.4
, we can denote

\displaystyle M_{*}:=

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in[0,T]}\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}

\displaystyle\;+\sup_{\eta\in[0,T]}\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}<+\infty,

\lambda_{*}:=\min\left\{\inf_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{1}(t)|_{*},\,\inf_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{2}(t)|_{*}\right\}>0,

\displaystyle N_{*}:=

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X_{1}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X_{1}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\;+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X_{2}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X_{2}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}}<+\infty.

and

\displaystyle Q_{*}:=

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in[0,T]}\|u_{1}(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{1}(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}(\nu\eta)^{\gamma}\|u_{1}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}}

\displaystyle\;+\sup_{\eta\in[0,T]}\|u_{2}(\eta)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{2}(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}(\nu\eta)^{\gamma}\|u_{2}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}}<+\infty.

Clearly, by (
2.18
) and (
2.19
),
(u_{1}-u_{2},X_{1}-X_{2})
satisfies on
[0,T]
that

\displaystyle(u_{1}-u_{2})(t)=

\displaystyle\;\big(u_{X_{1}}^{\nu}-u_{X_{2}}^{\nu}\big)(t)+\big(B_{\nu}[u_{1}]-B_{\nu}[u_{2}]\big)(t),

\displaystyle(X_{1}-X_{2})(t)=

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{I}\big[W[u_{1},X_{1}]-W[u_{2},X_{2}]\big](t).

For convenience, we define for
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
that (cf. (
4.9
))

\psi_{u}(t_{1},t_{2}):=\sup_{\eta\in(t_{1},t_{2}]}\big(\nu(\eta-t_{1})\big)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\|(u_{1}-u_{2})(\eta)\|_{M^{1,1-\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

and (cf. (
4.10
))

\displaystyle\psi_{X}(t_{1},t_{2}):=

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\|(X_{1}^{\prime}-X_{2}^{\prime})(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(t_{1},t_{2}]}(\eta-t_{1})^{\gamma}\|(X_{1}^{\prime}-X_{2}^{\prime})(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\;+\sup_{\eta\in(t_{1},t_{2})}(\eta-t_{1})^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}(X_{1}-X_{2})(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(t_{1},t_{2})}(\eta-t_{1})^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}(X_{1}-X_{2})(\eta)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}.

Given the estimates for
(u_{i},X_{i})
, they are well-defined functions.

Take
T_{0}\in(0,T]
with
T_{0}\leq 1
, such that for any
t\in[0,T_{0}]
,

(5.12)

\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{*}}{2},

and that, for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T_{0}]
,

(5.13)

\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t_{1})-X_{1}^{\prime}(t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{*}}{2},\quad\|X_{2}^{\prime}(t_{1})-X_{2}^{\prime}(t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{*}}{2}.

This can be achieved since
X_{1},X_{2}\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\mathbb{T}))
.

Arguing as before (see (
4.43
)), we find that

\displaystyle\psi_{u}(0,T_{0})\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left(T_{0}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}+T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\nu^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{*}^{-1-\gamma}M_{*}^{2}\cdot\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})

\displaystyle\;+CT_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q_{*}\cdot\psi_{u}(0,T_{0}),

where
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.

On the other hand
(see (
4.44
)),

\displaystyle\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})\leq

\displaystyle\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{0}]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{0}]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}.

We can analogously bound that (see (
4.49
))

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{0}]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{*},Q_{*},M_{*},N_{*})\big[\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})+\psi_{u}(0,T_{0})\big]\Big(T_{0}^{\frac{1}{2p}}+T_{0}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\Big).

Besides (cf. (
4.50
)), for any
t\in(0,T_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](t)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\|g_{X_{1}}(t)-g_{X_{2}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{*},Q_{*},M_{*},N_{*})\cdot\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})\cdot t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}-\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}Q_{*}M_{*}^{\gamma}\cdot\psi_{u}(0,T_{0})\cdot t^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}.

Thanks to Lemma
3.11
,

\displaystyle\;\|g_{X_{1}}(t)-g_{X_{2}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda_{*}^{-2}M_{*}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\cdot\Big[\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\lambda_{*}^{-1}\big(\|X^{\prime}_{1}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma/2}_{s}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma/2}_{s}}\big)\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)-X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{-\gamma}\lambda_{*}^{-2}M_{*}\cdot t^{\gamma/2}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)\cdot\lambda_{*}^{-1}M_{*}\psi_{X}(0,T_{0}),

with
C
depending on
\gamma
only.
This gives

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{0}]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|W[u_{1},X_{1}](\eta)-W[u_{2},X_{2}](\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,\lambda_{*},M_{*})\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{0}]}\eta^{\gamma/2}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big).

\displaystyle\;+C(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{*},Q_{*},M_{*},N_{*})\big[\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})+\psi_{u}(0,T_{0})\big]T_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}.

Plugging them into the estimate for
\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})
, and then combining with the estimate for
\psi_{u}(0,T_{0})
, we finally obtain that

\displaystyle\;\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})+\psi_{u}(0,T_{0})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\big(\psi_{X}(0,T_{0})+\psi_{u}(0,T_{0})\big)

\displaystyle\;\cdot\Big[C_{1}(\gamma,\lambda_{*},M_{*})\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{0}]}\eta^{\gamma/2}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\quad+C_{2}(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{*},Q_{*},M_{*},N_{*})\Big(T_{0}^{\frac{1}{2p}}+T_{0}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}+T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\Big].

By virtue of Lemma
5.6
and the fact
\gamma\in(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
, we may take
T_{0}
smaller if necessary, so that

\displaystyle\;C_{1}(\gamma,\lambda_{*},M_{*})\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{0}]}\eta^{\gamma/2}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma/2}}\big)

\displaystyle\;+C_{2}(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{*},Q_{*},M_{*},N_{*})\Big(T_{0}^{\frac{1}{2p}}+T_{0}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}+T_{0}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\leq\frac{1}{2}.

This implies that
X_{1}(s,t)\equiv X_{2}(s,t)
and
u_{1}(x,t)\equiv u_{2}(x,t)
for all
t\in[0,T_{0}]
.

If
T_{0}<T
, we shall repeat this argument with the new initial time
T_{0}
.
Let us only sketch it here.
We first derive that, with
T_{1}\in(T_{0},T]
to be chosen later, for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[T_{0},T_{1}]
with
t_{1}<t_{2}
,

\|X_{i}^{\prime}(t_{1})-X_{i}^{\prime}(t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq C\|X_{i}(t_{1})-X_{i}(t_{2})\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{\gamma}\|(X_{i}^{\prime}(t_{1}),X_{i}^{\prime}(t_{2}))\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{1-\gamma}\leq C|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\gamma}t_{1}^{-\gamma}M_{*}^{1-\gamma}N_{*}^{\gamma}.

This together with the fact
X_{1}(T_{0})=X_{2}(T_{0})
implies that there exists
\delta=\delta(T_{0},\lambda_{*},M_{*},N_{*})
, such that as long as
T_{1}\leq T_{0}+\delta
, (
5.12
) holds for any
t\in[T_{0},T_{1}]
and (
5.13
) holds for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[T_{0},T_{1}]
.
Now viewing
T_{0}
as the new initial data, the same argument as above leads to

\displaystyle\;\psi_{X}(T_{0},T_{1})+\psi_{u}(T_{0},T_{1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\big(\psi_{X}(T_{0},T_{1})+\psi_{u}(T_{0},T_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\;\cdot\Big[C_{3}(\gamma,\lambda_{*},M_{*})\sup_{\eta\in(T_{0},T_{1}]}(\eta-T_{0})^{\gamma/2}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\quad+C_{4}(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{*},Q_{*},M_{*},N_{*})\Big((T_{1}-T_{0})^{\frac{1}{2p}}+(T_{1}-T_{0})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}+(T_{1}-T_{0})^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\Big].

This time, instead of applying Lemma
5.6
, we observe that

\sup_{\eta\in(T_{0},T_{1}]}(\eta-T_{0})^{\gamma/2}\left(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\right)\leq C\left(\frac{T_{1}-T_{0}}{T_{0}}\right)^{\gamma/2}M_{*}.

Hence, there exists
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(T_{0},\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{*},Q_{*},M_{*},N_{*})\leq\delta
, such that as long as
T_{1}\leq T_{0}+\delta_{0}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\;C_{3}(\gamma,\lambda_{*},M_{*})\sup_{\eta\in(T_{0},T_{1}]}(\eta-T_{0})^{\gamma/2}\big(\|X_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{2}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)

\displaystyle\;+C_{4}(\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda_{*},Q_{*},M_{*},N_{*})\Big((T_{1}-T_{0})^{\frac{1}{2p}}+(T_{1}-T_{0})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}+(T_{1}-T_{0})^{\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\leq\frac{1}{2}.

This gives
\psi_{X}(T_{0},T_{1})+\psi_{u}(T_{0},T_{1})=0
,
and thus
X_{1}(t)\equiv X_{2}(t)
and
u_{1}(t)\equiv u_{2}(t)
in
t\in[T_{0},T_{1}]
.
Then we repeat this argument, and each time we may extend the time interval by
\delta_{0}
if it has not exceeded
T
.
This leads to the conclusion that
X_{1}(t)\equiv X_{2}(t)
and
u_{1}(t)\equiv u_{2}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
.
∎

The next proposition is concerned with continuation of the mild solutions.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
and
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, which satisfy
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
Suppose that for some
T>0
,
(u,X)
is a mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
2.1
.
Then it can be extended to a longer time interval, i.e., for some
\tilde{T}>T
, there exists
(\tilde{u},\tilde{X})
forming a mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,\tilde{T}]
and satisfying
(u,X)=(\tilde{u},\tilde{X})
on
[0,T]
.

Proof.

Fix
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)\cap(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
.
By the definition of the mild solution as well as Lemma
5.4
,
u(T)\in L_{x}^{p}\cap C_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
X(T)\in C_{s}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
, and they satisfy
\mathrm{div}\,u(T)=0
and
|X(T)|_{*}>0
.
Denote

M_{1}:=\|X^{\prime}(T)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},\quad\lambda_{1}:=|X(T)|_{*}>0,\quad Q_{1}:=\|u(T)\|_{L_{x}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|u(T)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\lambda_{1}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{1}^{2}.

Then we apply Proposition
4.1
with the same
p
and
\gamma
to construct a local mild solution starting from the initial data
(u(T),X(T))
.
More precisely, there exists
T_{1}\in(0,1)
and a mild solution
(u_{1},X_{1})
on
[0,T_{1}]
to (
1.7
)-(
1.10
) with the initial condition
u_{1}(x,0)=u(x,T)
, and
X_{1}(s,0)=X(s,T)
.
Thanks to Proposition
4.1
, it is known that

•

\|u_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq CQ_{1},\quad\|X_{1}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}<+\infty,\quad\inf_{t\in[0,T_{1}]}|X_{1}(t)|_{*}>\lambda_{1}/2,

where the constant
C
only depends on
p
(see (
4.24
) and the remark that follows it);

•

and with
\sigma:=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p})\in(0,\gamma)
,

(5.14)

t^{\gamma}\|X_{1}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+\|X_{1}(t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X(T)\|_{C_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 4\big(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\big),

where
\rho(t)
is defined in terms of
X_{1}(s,0)=X(s,T)
(cf. (
4.3
)):

\rho(t)=\rho_{X(T),\gamma}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X^{\prime}(T)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}.

Our goal is to show

(5.15)

\|X_{1}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}C^{1/p}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}<+\infty.

Since
X(T)\in C_{s}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
, we find that

\rho(t)\leq t^{\gamma}M_{1}
.
Combining this with (
5.14
), we derive that, for any
t\in(0,T_{1}]
,

(5.16)

\begin{split}\|X_{1}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq&\;\big\|X_{1}(t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X(T)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}+\big\|e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X(T)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\big\|X_{1}(t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X(T)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}}^{\frac{\sigma}{\gamma}}\big\|X_{1}(t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X(T)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}}^{1-\frac{\sigma}{\gamma}}+\|X(T)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\sigma}}\\
\leq&\;C\left(\|X_{1}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}}+\|X(T)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\right)^{\frac{\sigma}{\gamma}}\big(\rho(t)+t^{\sigma}\big)^{1-\frac{\sigma}{\gamma}}+\|X(T)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}}\\
\leq&\;C\left[t^{-\gamma}\big(t^{\gamma}M_{1}+t^{\sigma}\big)+M_{1}\right]^{\frac{\sigma}{\gamma}}\big(t^{\gamma}M_{1}+t^{\sigma}\big)^{1-\frac{\sigma}{\gamma}}+M_{1}\\
\leq&\;Ct^{-\sigma}\big(t^{\gamma}M_{1}+t^{\sigma}\big)+M_{1},\end{split}

where
C
is a universal constant.
Since
\sigma<\gamma
and
T_{1}\leq 1
,

(5.17)

\|X_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\sigma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(1+M_{1}),

where
C
is universal.
If
\sigma\geq\frac{1}{p}
, this already implies (
5.15
).
In what follows, we shall handle the case
\sigma<\frac{1}{p}
.

First, we show
u_{1}\in L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
Recall that, for
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

(5.18)

u_{1}(t)=u_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u(T)+B_{\nu}[u_{1}](t).

By Lemma
3.2
and Lemma
A.1
, for
t\in[0,T_{1}]
with
T_{1}\leq 1
,

\big\|u_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u(T)\big\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\lambda_{1}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\|X_{1}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}+\|u(T)\|_{L^{p}_{x}}\leq C(p,\lambda_{1},M_{1},Q_{1}),

and

\big\|u_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u(T)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\lambda_{1}^{-1}\|X_{1}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\sigma}}^{2}+\|u(T)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C(p,\lambda_{1},M_{1},Q_{1}).

By interpolation, for any
q\in[p,\infty]
,

(5.19)

\big\|u_{X_{1}}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u(T)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(p,\lambda_{1},M_{1},Q_{1}).

On the other hand, by (
3.6
) in Lemma
3.8
, for any
q,r\in[p,\infty]
satisfying
\frac{1}{q}\in(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{p}]
and
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{r}\leq 1
,

\|B_{\nu}[u_{1}](t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{1-\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}}\|(u_{1}\otimes u_{1})(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{pr}{p+r}}},

where
C
depends on
p
,
q
, and
r
.
Combining this with (
5.18
) and (
5.19
) yields that

\|u_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L^{q}_{x}}\leq C(p,\lambda_{1},M_{1},Q_{1})+C\nu^{-1}(\nu T_{1})^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L_{x}^{p}}\|u_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L_{x}^{r}}.

By choosing suitable pairs of
q
and
r
, we can bootstrap as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
to show that

(5.20)

\|u_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(p,\nu,\lambda_{1},M_{1},Q_{1}).

We omit the details.

In order to show (
5.15
) in the case
\sigma<\frac{1}{p}
, one may proceed as in the proofs of Lemma
5.1
, Lemma
5.3
, and Lemma
5.4
to further raise the regularity of
(u_{1},X_{1})
, but we shall present a different approach here.

Let
q\in(2,\infty)
such that
\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p})=\sigma
.
By the assumption
\sigma<\frac{1}{p}
, we have
q>p
, so
u(T)\in L_{x}^{p}\cap C_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
implies
u(T)\in L_{x}^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
Take
\gamma^{\prime}\in[\frac{2}{q},1)\cap(1-\frac{2}{q},1)
such that
\gamma^{\prime}\geq\gamma
; we have
X(T)\in C^{\gamma^{\prime}}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
thanks to Lemma
5.4
.
Now we may apply Proposition
4.1
with the new parameters
q
and
\gamma^{\prime}
to construct another local mild solution starting from
(u(T),X(T))
.
This time we choose the parameter
\sigma
in the definition (
4.7
) of
\mathcal{X}
to be
\frac{1}{p}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{q})
; this is valid due to Remark
4.1
.
In this way, we obtain
\tilde{T}_{1}\in(0,1)
and a mild solution
(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{X}_{1})
on
[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
to (
1.7
)-(
1.10
) with the initial condition

\tilde{u}_{1}(x,0)=u(x,T)
, and
\tilde{X}_{1}(s,0)=X(s,T)
.
We also know that

\|\tilde{u}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{T}_{1}}L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\tilde{X}_{1}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tilde{T}_{1}}L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}<+\infty,\quad\inf_{t\in[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]}|\tilde{X}_{1}(t)|_{*}>0,

and for
t\in(0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
,

t^{\gamma^{\prime}}\|\tilde{X}_{1}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T})}+\|\tilde{X}_{1}(t)-e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X(T)\|_{C_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 4\big(\tilde{\rho}(t)+t^{1/p}\big),

where

\tilde{\rho}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\gamma^{\prime}}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X^{\prime}(T)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma^{\prime}}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}.

By interpolation,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,\tilde{T}_{1}]}\eta^{\frac{1}{q}}\|\tilde{X}_{1}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/q}(\mathbb{T})}<+\infty.

Furthermore, by arguing as in (
5.16
) and (
5.17
), we find that

(5.21)

\tilde{X}_{1}\in L^{\infty}_{\tilde{T}_{1}}C^{1,\frac{1}{p}}_{s}(\mathbb{T}).

On the other hand, the known bounds for
(u_{1},X_{1})
and (
5.20
) give

\|u_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|X_{1}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{1}}L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{1}]}\eta^{\frac{1}{q}}\|X_{1}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/q}(\mathbb{T})}<+\infty,\quad\inf_{t\in[0,T_{1}]}|X_{1}(t)|_{*}>0.

Therefore, both
(u_{1},X_{1})
and
(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{X}_{1})
are mild solutions on
[0,T_{1}]\cap[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
to (
1.7
)-(
1.10
) with the initial data
(u(x,T),X(s,T))\in L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times C^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
in the sense of Definition
2.1
.
By Proposition
5.1
, we must have
\tilde{X}_{1}(t)\equiv X_{1}(t)
on
[0,T_{1}]\cap[0,\tilde{T}_{1}]
.
Therefore, in the case
T_{1}\leq\tilde{T}_{1}
, (
5.21
) implies (
5.15
), while if
T_{1}>\tilde{T}_{1}
, (
5.15
) follows from (
5.14
) and (
5.21
).

In either case, we have shown (
5.15
).
Let
\tilde{T}:=T+T_{1}
, and

\tilde{u}(x,t):=\begin{cases}u(x,t),&\mbox{if }t\in[0,T],\\
u_{1}(x,t-T),&\mbox{if }t\in[T,\tilde{T}],\end{cases}\quad\tilde{X}(s,t):=\begin{cases}X(s,t),&\mbox{if }t\in[0,T],\\
X_{1}(s,t-T),&\mbox{if }t\in[T,\tilde{T}].\end{cases}

It is straightforward to verify that
(\tilde{u},\tilde{X})
is a mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,\tilde{T}]
, which is an extension of
(u,X)
.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

The lifespan
T_{1}
of the extended part
(u_{1},X_{1})
only depends on
\gamma
,
p
,
\nu
,
|X(T)|_{*}
,
\|u(T)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
, and
\|X^{\prime}(T)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}
.
Indeed, since
X(T)\in C^{1,\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
, the corresponding
\rho
and
\phi
defined in terms of
X(T)
(see (
4.3
) and (
4.5
)) should satisfy

\rho(t)\leq t^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(T)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},\quad\phi(t)\leq Ct^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(T)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},

where
C
only depends on
\gamma
.
Then the claim follows from Proposition
4.1
and Remark
4.4
.

A corollary of Proposition
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
is the existence and characterization of a maximal mild solution (see Definition
2.2
).

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
and
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, which satisfy
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
Then there exists a unique
T_{*}\in(0,+\infty]
and a unique maximal mild solution
(u,X)
to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) with its maximal lifespan being
T_{*}
.

Moreover, if
T_{*}<+\infty
, at least one of the following scenarios would occur:

(a)

\limsup_{t\to T_{*}^{-}}\|u(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=+\infty
;

(b)

\liminf_{t\to T_{*}^{-}}|X(t)|_{*}=0
;

(c)

For any increasing sequence
t_{k}\to T_{*}^{-}
,
\{X^{\prime}(t_{k})\}_{k}
is not convergent in
C(\mathbb{T})
.
As a result,
X^{\prime}([0,T_{*}))
, which is the image of
[0,T_{*})
under the mapping
t\mapsto X^{\prime}(\cdot,t)
, is not pre-compact in
C(\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

The existence and uniqueness of
T_{*}
and the maximal solution
(u,X)
immediately follows from Proposition
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
.
We will only prove the characterizations of solution when it approaches the finite blow-up time
T_{*}
.

We prove by contradiction.
Suppose none of the above-mentioned three scenarios occurs as
t\to T_{*}^{-}
.
Take an increasing sequence
t_{k}\to T_{*}^{-}
, such that
\{X^{\prime}(t_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is convergent in
C(\mathbb{T})
.
Since the limit has mean zero, we shall denote it as
X_{\circ}^{\prime}(s)
;
without loss of generality, we also assume
X_{\circ}
to have mean zero.
By assumption, with abuse of the notations,

Q:=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|u(t_{k})\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}<+\infty,\quad\lambda:=\inf_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}|X(t_{k})|_{*}>0.

It is not difficult to show
|X_{\circ}|_{*}\geq\lambda
.
Note that the mean of
X_{\circ}
is irrelevant when defining
|X_{\circ}|_{*}
.

Take
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)\cap(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
.
Let
\rho_{k}
and
\phi_{k}
be defined in terms of
X(t_{k})
as in (
4.3
) and (
4.5
):

\displaystyle\rho_{k}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X^{\prime}(t_{k})\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},\quad\phi_{k}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\big\|X(t_{k})-e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X(t_{k})\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}.

Also define

\displaystyle\rho_{\circ}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{\circ}^{\prime}\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},\quad\phi_{\circ}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\big\|X_{\circ}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{\circ}\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}.

They are all continuous increasing functions.
Note again that
\rho_{\circ}
and
\phi_{\circ}
do not rely on the mean of
X_{\circ}
.
Since
\lim_{k\to+\infty}\|X^{\prime}(t_{k})-X^{\prime}_{\circ}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}=0
, we find that

M:=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}\|X^{\prime}(t_{k})\|_{C_{s}(\mathbb{T})}<+\infty,

and

(5.22)

\rho_{k}(t)\rightrightarrows\rho_{\circ}(t),\mbox{ and }\phi_{k}(t)\rightrightarrows\phi_{\circ}(t)\mbox{ for }t\in[0,+\infty).

Now we apply Proposition
4.1
with the parameters
p
and
\gamma
to construct local solutions starting from the initial datum
(u(t_{k}),X(t_{k}))
.
Namely, for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, there exists
T_{k}>0
and mild solutions
(u_{k},X_{k})
on
[0,T_{k}]
to (
1.7
)-(
1.10
) with the initial condition
u_{k}(x,0)=u(x,t_{k})
, and
X_{k}(s,0)=X(s,t_{k})
.
By virtue of Remark
4.4
,

T_{k}
should depend on
\gamma,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda,\,Q
, and
M
, and additionally, we need
(\rho_{k}(T_{k})+T_{k}^{\sigma})
and
\phi_{k}(T_{k})
to be smaller than some constants that depend on
\gamma,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda,\,Q
, and
M
.
By (
5.22
), we may choose
\{T_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
, such that
T_{\circ}:=\inf_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}T_{k}>0
.

Now take
k
sufficiently large so that
T_{*}-t_{k}<T_{\circ}
.
Define

\tilde{u}(x,t):=\begin{cases}u(x,t),&\mbox{if }t\in[0,t_{k}],\\
u_{k}(x,t-t_{k}),&\mbox{if }t\in[t_{k},t_{k}+T_{k}],\end{cases}\quad\tilde{X}(s,t):=\begin{cases}X(s,t),&\mbox{if }t\in[0,t_{k}],\\
X_{k}(s,t-t_{k}),&\mbox{if }t\in[t_{k},t_{k}+T_{k}].\end{cases}

By arguing as in Proposition
5.2
, we can show that
(\tilde{u},\tilde{X})
gives a mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,t_{k}+T_{k}]
.
Since
t_{k}+T_{k}\geq t_{k}+T_{\circ}>T_{*}
for sufficiently large
k
, this contradicts with the maximality of
T_{*}
.

This completes the proof.
∎

5.3.
Higher-order regularity

As is introduced at the beginning of this section, we still assume
(u,X)
to be the unique mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,T]
, and let
M
,
\lambda
, and
Q
be defined in (
5.1
)-(
5.3
), respectively.
In this subsection, we will show that
X=X(s,t)
will instantly become
C_{s}^{2,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and
u=u(x,t)
will instantly gain Lipschitz regularity in space, with their high-order norms admitting time-singularities of optimal orders at
t=0
.

We first derive a higher-order estimates for
H_{X}^{\nu}
, which is an extension of Lemma
4.2
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
.
Suppose that
X\in L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{s}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
, satisfying that
|X|_{*}\geq\lambda
and
\partial_{t}X\in L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
.
Denote

\displaystyle Z[\gamma,\nu,\lambda,X,t]:=

\displaystyle\;\nu^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\gamma}}^{1-\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+(\nu t)^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}t\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left(\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\gamma}}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right).

Then

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{2\gamma-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;CZ[\gamma,\nu,\lambda,X,t],

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}Z[\gamma,\nu,\lambda,X,t],

and

\|H_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,2\gamma-1}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\left(\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}^{2\gamma}+(\nu t)^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}^{2\gamma-1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\right)Z[\gamma,\nu,\lambda,X,t],

where the constants
C
depend on
\gamma
.

Proof.

By (
3.3
) in Lemma
3.6
, with the parameters there taken as
(\alpha,\gamma^{\prime},\mu_{0},\mu,\mu^{\prime}):=(2\gamma,\gamma,0,0,0)
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{2\gamma-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left[(\nu t)^{-\gamma}\min\left(\lambda^{-1},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}(\nu t)^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1}\right]t^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\gamma}}^{1-\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C\left[(\nu t)^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\min\left(\lambda^{-\gamma},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\right)\lambda^{-1}+\mathds{1}_{\{\nu t\geq 4\lambda^{2}\}}(\nu t)^{-1}\lambda^{-2\gamma}\right]

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot t\left(\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\gamma}}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1}t^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\gamma}}^{1-\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}t

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left(\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\gamma}}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;CZ[\gamma,\nu,\lambda,X,t],

where
C
depends on
\gamma
.

Similarly, by (
3.2
) in Lemma
3.6
, with the parameters there taken as
(\gamma^{\prime},\mu_{0},\mu,\mu^{\prime}):=(\gamma,0,0,0)
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla h_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda^{-1}t^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\gamma}}^{1-\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{2-\gamma}{2}}\min\left(\lambda^{-\gamma},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\right)\ln\left(2+\frac{\nu t}{\lambda^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\lambda^{-1}t\left(\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{s}^{\gamma}}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{t}X\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}Z[\gamma,\nu,\lambda,X,t],

where
C
depends on
\gamma
.
Here we used the fact that

\min\left(\lambda^{-\gamma},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\right)\ln\left(2+\frac{\nu t}{\lambda^{2}}\right)\leq C\lambda^{-\gamma}\left(1+\frac{\nu t}{\lambda^{2}}\right)^{-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\ln\left(2+\frac{\nu t}{\lambda^{2}}\right)\leq C\lambda^{-\gamma}

where
C
only depends on
\gamma
.

The estimate for
H_{X}^{\nu}
follows from Lemma
3.6
.
∎

The next lemma is concerned with higher-order estimates for
B_{\nu}[u]
.

Lemma 5.8
.

For any
\beta\in(0,1)
and
t\in[0,T]
,

(\nu t)^{-\beta/2}\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\beta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\nu^{-1}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{C}_{x}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
C
depends on
\beta
.

Proof.

It follows from

\displaystyle\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}}\leq

\displaystyle\;\int_{0}^{t}\big\|\nabla e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}\mathbb{P}\mathrm{div}\,(u\otimes u)(\tau)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{0}^{t}(\nu(t-\tau))^{-\frac{2-\beta}{2}}\|(u\otimes u)(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{x}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\beta/2}\sup_{\tau\in(0,t)}\|(u\otimes u)(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{x}},

and by Lemma
3.15
,

\displaystyle\|B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\beta}_{x}}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left|\sup_{\tau\in(0,t)}(t-\tau)^{\frac{2-\beta}{2}}(\nu(t-\tau))^{-\frac{2-\beta}{2}}\|(u\otimes u)(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{x}}\right|^{1-\beta}

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left|\sup_{\tau\in(0,t)}(t-\tau)^{\frac{3-\beta}{2}}(\nu(t-\tau))^{-\frac{3-\beta}{2}}\|(u\otimes u)(\tau)\|_{C^{\beta}_{x}}\right|^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-1}\sup_{\tau\in(0,t)}\|(u\otimes u)(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{x}}.

∎

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)\cap[\frac{2}{p},1)
.
For any
t\in(0,T]
,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{2\gamma}\|X^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{2\gamma-1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{2\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{2\gamma-1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q),

where as before,
C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)
denotes a constant depending on
T,\,\gamma,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda,\,M
, and
Q
.

Proof.

Take an arbitrary
t_{0}\in(0,T]
.
Since
\gamma\geq\frac{2}{p}
, the results in Section
5.1
imply that

•

X\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T}\times[t_{0}/2,T])
and
u\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[t_{0}/2,T])
;

•

\inf_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,T]}|X(\eta)|_{*}\geq\lambda
, and

\displaystyle\;t_{0}^{\gamma}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,T]}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+t_{0}^{\gamma}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,T)}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+t_{0}^{1/p}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,T)}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q);

•

and, moreover,

\displaystyle t_{0}^{1/p}\sup_{\tau\in[t_{0}/2,T]}\|u(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+t_{0}^{\gamma}\sup_{\tau\in[t_{0}/2,T]}\|u(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

It is not difficult to verify by definition that,
(u,X)
satisfies on
t\in[t_{0}/2,T]
that (cf. (
2.13
)-(
2.15
), and (
2.18
)-(
2.21
))

(5.23)

\displaystyle u(t)=

\displaystyle\;u_{X(t)}+\tilde{h}_{X,t_{0}/2}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu(t-t_{0}/2)\Delta}(u(t_{0}/2)-u_{X(t)})+\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{0}/2}[u](t),

(5.24)

\displaystyle X(t)=

\displaystyle\;e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-t_{0}/2)\Lambda}X(t_{0}/2)+\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{t_{0}/2}\big[\tilde{W}[u,X;\nu,u(t_{0}/2),t_{0}/2]\big](t),

and it holds on
[t_{0}/2,T)
that (cf. (
5.7
))

(5.25)

\partial_{t}X(s,t)=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda X(s,t)+\tilde{W}[u,X;\nu,u(t_{0}/2),t_{0}/2](t)=u(X(s,t),t).

Here we denoted, with
0\leq t_{*}\leq t
,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{X,t_{*}}^{\nu}(t):=

\displaystyle\;-\nu\int_{t_{*}}^{t}\Delta e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}(u_{X(\tau)}-u_{X(t)})\,d\tau,

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{X,t_{*}}^{\nu}(t):=

\displaystyle\;\tilde{h}_{X,t_{*}}^{\nu}(t)\circ X(t),

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{*}}[u](t):=

\displaystyle\;-\int_{t_{*}}^{t}e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}\mathbb{P}(u\cdot\nabla u)(\tau)\,d\tau,

\displaystyle\tilde{W}[u,X;\nu,\tilde{u}_{0},t_{*}](t):=

\displaystyle\;g_{X}(t)+\tilde{H}_{X,t_{*}}^{\nu}(t)+\big[e^{\nu(t-t_{*})\Delta}(\tilde{u}_{0}-u_{X(t)})+\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{*}}[u]\big]\circ X(t),

and

\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{t_{*}}[\tilde{W}](t):=\int_{t_{*}}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-\tau)\Lambda}\tilde{W}(\tau)\,d\tau.

In what follows, with
(u,X)
and
t_{0}
given, we shall write
\tilde{W}[u,X;\nu,u(t_{0}/2),t_{0}/2]
as
\tilde{W}[u,X]
or even
\tilde{W}
for brevity.

Denote
\alpha:=2\gamma-1
.
We shall derive a
C^{2,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
-estimate for
X(t_{0})
using the new representation.
By (
5.24
), Lemma
A.2
, and Lemma
A.4
with
\varphi(t)=t^{-\gamma}
,

(5.26)

\begin{split}\|X^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})\|_{C^{\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq&\;C\big\|e^{-\frac{t_{0}}{8}\Lambda}X^{\prime}(t_{0}/2)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+C\big\|\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{t_{0}/2}\big[\tilde{W}[u,X]\big](t_{0})\big\|_{\dot{C}^{2,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;Ct_{0}^{-\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(t_{0}/2)\|_{C^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+Ct_{0}^{-\gamma}\sup_{\tau\in[t_{0}/2,t_{0}]}(\tau-t_{0}/2)^{\gamma}\|\tilde{W}[u,X](\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}.\end{split}

By Lemma
B.1
, for any
t\in[\frac{t_{0}}{2},t_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{W}[u,X](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\|g_{X}(t)\|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{s}}+\|\tilde{H}_{X,t_{0}/2}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{s}}

\displaystyle\;+\big\|e^{\nu(t-t_{0}/2)\Delta}(u(t_{0}/2)-u_{X(t)})+\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{0}/2}[u](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}_{s}}

\displaystyle\;+\big\|e^{\nu(t-t_{0}/2)\Delta}(u(t_{0}/2)-u_{X(t)})+\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{0}/2}[u](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{x}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{1+\alpha}.

We shall estimate these terms one by one.

By Lemma
3.10
, for any
t\in[\frac{t_{0}}{2},t_{0}]
,

\|g_{X}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{2}\leq C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-2\gamma}.

With
t_{0}/2
viewed as the new initial time, we apply Lemma
5.7
to obtain that, for any
t\in[\frac{t_{0}}{2},t_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\;\|\tilde{H}_{X,t_{0}/2}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left(\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2\gamma}+(\nu(t-t_{0}/2))^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t]}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{2\gamma-1}_{s}}\right)

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left[\nu^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t]}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}}^{1-\gamma}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t]}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{\gamma}\right.

\displaystyle\;\quad+(\nu(t-t_{0}/2))^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}(t-t_{0}/2)\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\;\left.\qquad\cdot\left(\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t]}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t]}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t]}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)\left(1+(t-t_{0}/2)^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}t_{0}^{-(2\gamma-1)}\right)\left(t_{0}^{-\gamma}+(t-t_{0}/2)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}t_{0}^{-\gamma-\frac{1}{p}}\right).

Since
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
and
t_{0}\leq T
, for
t\in[\frac{t_{0}}{2},t_{0}]
,

\|\tilde{H}_{X,t_{0}/2}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-2\gamma}.

By Lemma
3.1
(with
\gamma=\frac{1}{p}
there) and Lemma
A.1
, for
t\in[\frac{t_{0}}{2},t_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\;(\nu(t-t_{0}/2))^{1/2}\big\|e^{\nu(t-t_{0}/2)\Delta}(u(t_{0}/2)-u_{X(t)})\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\;+(\nu(t-t_{0}/2))^{\gamma}\big\|e^{\nu(t-t_{0}/2)\Delta}(u(t_{0}/2)-u_{X(t)})\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left(\|u(t_{0}/2)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|u_{X(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left[t_{0}^{-\frac{1}{p}}C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)+t^{-\frac{1}{p}}\cdot\lambda^{-1}M^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-1/p}.

So for
t\in[\frac{t_{0}}{2},t_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|e^{\nu(t-t_{0}/2)\Delta}(u(t_{0}/2)-u_{X(t)})\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}_{s}}

\displaystyle\;+\big\|e^{\nu(t-t_{0}/2)\Delta}(u(t_{0}/2)-u_{X(t)})\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{x}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)(t-t_{0}/2)^{-\gamma}t_{0}^{-1/p}\left[(t-t_{0}/2)^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}t_{0}^{-\alpha}+1\right].

By Lemma
5.8
and the interpolation inequality,

\displaystyle\;(\nu(t-t_{0}/2))^{-\alpha/2}\big\|\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{0}/2}[u](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\big\|\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{0}/2}[u](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-1}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t]}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t]}\|u(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{-1}\cdot t_{0}^{-\frac{1}{p}}C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)\cdot t_{0}^{-\alpha-\frac{1}{p}(1-\frac{\alpha}{\gamma})}C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q),

and thus, for
t\in[\frac{t_{0}}{2},t_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{0}/2}[u](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}_{s}}+\big\|\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{0}/2}[u](t)\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{x}}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left[(\nu(t-t_{0}/2))^{\alpha/2}\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}_{s}}+\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{1+\alpha}\right]\nu^{-1}\cdot t_{0}^{-\alpha-\frac{1}{p}(2-\frac{\alpha}{\gamma})}C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)\left(t_{0}^{-\alpha/2}+1\right)t_{0}^{-\alpha-\frac{1}{p}(2-\frac{\alpha}{\gamma})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-2\gamma}.

In the last line, we used the facts that
\gamma\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
,
\alpha=2\gamma-1
, and
t_{0}\leq T
.
Combining all these bounds into the estimate for
\tilde{W}
, we find that, for
t\in[\frac{t_{0}}{2},t_{0}]
,

(5.27)

\begin{split}\|\tilde{W}[u,X](t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq&\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-2\gamma}\\
&\;+C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)(t-t_{0}/2)^{-\gamma}t_{0}^{-1/p}\left(t_{0}^{-\gamma+\frac{1}{2}}+1\right).\end{split}

Since
p>2
,
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
, and
t_{0}\leq T
,

\displaystyle\;t_{0}^{-\gamma}\sup_{\tau\in[t_{0}/2,t_{0}]}(\tau-t_{0}/2)^{\gamma}\|\tilde{W}[u,X](\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-2\gamma}+C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-\gamma}\cdot t_{0}^{-1/p}\left(t_{0}^{-\gamma+\frac{1}{2}}+1\right).

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-2\gamma}.

Hence, by (
5.26
),

\|X^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})\|_{C^{\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-2\gamma}.

Note that
t_{0}\in(0,T]
is arbitrary.
This together with (
5.25
) and (
5.27
) that, with
t_{0}\in(0,T)
,

\|\partial_{t}X^{\prime}(t_{0})\|_{C^{\alpha}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\left(\|\Lambda X^{\prime}(t_{0})\|_{C^{\alpha}_{s}}+\|\tilde{W}[u,X](t_{0})\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}_{s}}\right)\leq C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-2\gamma}.

This proves the desired estimate.
∎

To study the higher-order regularity of
u
, we need the following Lipschitz estimate for the Stokes flow field
u_{X}
under the assumption that
X\in C_{s}^{2,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
, which can be viewed as an extension of Lemma
3.1
.
Let us mention that a similar estimate on the log-Lipschitz regularity of
u_{X}
was established under slightly weaker assumptions in
[
53
, Lemma 2.2]
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Suppose
X=X(s)\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
|X|_{*}=\lambda>0
.
Then
u_{X}=u_{X}(x)
is Lipschitz in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, satisfying that, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
,

|u_{X}(x)-u_{X}(y)|\leq C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{1+\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}|x-y|,

where
C
depends on
\alpha
.

Proof.

It suffices to bound
\nabla u_{X}(x)
when
x\not\in X(\mathbb{T})
.
This is because Lemma
3.1
implies the continuity of
u_{X}
across
X(\mathbb{T})
, and the points on
X(\mathbb{T})
can always be approximated by those outside
X(\mathbb{T})
.

For
\delta\in(0,1]
to be chosen later, we take
\chi_{\delta}:\mathbb{T}\to[0,1]
to be an even smooth cut-off function, satisfying that
\chi_{\delta}(s)\equiv 1
in
[-\delta,\delta]
,
\chi_{\delta}(s)\equiv 0
in
\mathbb{T}\setminus[-2\delta,2\delta]
,
\chi_{\delta}
is decreasing on
[0,\pi]
, and
|\chi_{\delta}^{\prime}(s)|\leq C\delta^{-1}
for some universal
C>0
.

By (
2.7
), with
s_{x}
defined as in (
2.8
) and with
\delta\in(0,1]
to be determined,

\displaystyle\;\partial_{k}u_{X,i}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{s_{x}-\pi}^{s_{x}+\pi}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(x-X(s^{\prime}))X_{j}^{\prime\prime}(s^{\prime})\cdot\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime}-s_{x})\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+\int_{s_{x}-\pi}^{s_{x}+\pi}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(x-X(s^{\prime}))X_{j}^{\prime\prime}(s^{\prime})\big[1-\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime}-s_{x})\big]\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{s_{x}-\pi}^{s_{x}+\pi}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(x-X(s^{\prime}))\big(X_{j}^{\prime\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}(s_{x})\big)\cdot\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime}-s_{x})\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+X_{j}^{\prime\prime}(s_{x})\int_{s_{x}-\pi}^{s_{x}+\pi}\Big[\partial_{k}G_{ij}(x-X(s^{\prime}))-\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(x-X(s_{x})-(s^{\prime}-s_{x})X^{\prime}(s_{x})\big)\Big]\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime}-s_{x})\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+X_{j}^{\prime\prime}(s_{x})\int_{s_{x}-\pi}^{s_{x}+\pi}\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(x-X(s_{x})-(s^{\prime}-s_{x})X^{\prime}(s_{x})\big)\cdot\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime}-s_{x})\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+\int_{s_{x}-\pi}^{s_{x}+\pi}\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(x-X(s^{\prime}))X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big[1-\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime}-s_{x})\big]\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+\int_{s_{x}-\pi}^{s_{x}+\pi}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(x-X(s^{\prime}))X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\cdot\chi_{\delta}^{\prime}(s^{\prime}-s_{x})\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\;V_{1}+V_{2}+V_{3}+V_{4}+V_{5}.

Here we performed integration by parts to obtain the last two terms
V_{4}
and
V_{5}
.

It is not difficult to verify by (
1.17
) that (cf. Lemma
3.12
), for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

|\nabla^{k}G(x)|\leq C_{k}|x|^{-k},

and for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

|\nabla^{k}G(x)-\nabla^{k}G(y)|\leq C_{k}|x-y|\big(|x|^{-k-1}+|y|^{-k-1}\big).

Lemma
3.13
implies that,

|x-X(s^{\prime})|\geq\lambda|s_{x}-s^{\prime}|.

Moreover, with
d=d(x)
defined as in (
2.2
),

|x-X(s_{x})-(s^{\prime}-s_{x})X^{\prime}(s_{x})|=\big(d^{2}+(s^{\prime}-s_{x})^{2}|X^{\prime}(s_{x})|^{2}\big)^{1/2}\geq\lambda|s_{x}-s^{\prime}|,

and

|X(s^{\prime})-X(s_{x})-(s^{\prime}-s_{x})X^{\prime}(s_{x})|=\left|\int_{s_{x}}^{s^{\prime}}X^{\prime}(s^{\prime\prime})-X^{\prime}(s_{x})\,ds^{\prime\prime}\right|\leq C|s^{\prime}-s_{x}|^{1+\alpha}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}.

Using these estimates, we can derive that

\displaystyle|V_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{s_{x}-2\delta}^{s_{x}+2\delta}|x-X(s^{\prime})|^{-1}|s^{\prime}-s_{x}|^{\alpha}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}\int_{s_{x}-2\delta}^{s_{x}+2\delta}\lambda^{-1}|s^{\prime}-s_{x}|^{-1+\alpha}\,ds^{\prime}\leq C\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}\delta^{\alpha},

where
C
depends on
\alpha
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle|V_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\;C\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\int_{s_{x}-2\delta}^{s_{x}+2\delta}|s^{\prime}-s_{x}|^{1+\alpha}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\big(|x-X(s^{\prime})|^{-2}+|x-X(s_{x})-(s^{\prime}-s_{x})X^{\prime}(s_{x})|^{-2}\big)\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\int_{s_{x}-2\delta}^{s_{x}+2\delta}|s^{\prime}-s_{x}|^{1+\alpha}\cdot\lambda^{-2}|s^{\prime}-s_{x}|^{-2}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}\delta^{\alpha}.

In the last line, we applied the interpolation inequality.
Moreover,

\displaystyle|V_{4}|\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{[s_{x}-\pi,s_{x}+\pi]\setminus[s_{x}-\delta,s_{x}+\delta]}|x-X(s^{\prime})|^{-2}|X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|^{2}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{[s_{x}-\pi,s_{x}+\pi]\setminus[s_{x}-\delta,s_{x}+\delta]}\lambda^{-2}|s^{\prime}-s_{x}|^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\,ds^{\prime}\leq C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\delta^{-1}.

and

\displaystyle|V_{5}|\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{s_{x}-\pi}^{s_{x}+\pi}|x-X(s^{\prime})|^{-1}|X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\cdot|\chi_{\delta}^{\prime}(s^{\prime}-s_{x})|\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{[s_{x}-2\delta,s_{x}+2\delta]\setminus[s_{x}-\delta,s_{x}+\delta]}\lambda^{-1}|s^{\prime}-s_{x}|^{-1}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\cdot\delta^{-1}\,ds^{\prime}\leq C\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\delta^{-1}.

We shall handle
|V_{3}|
in a more explicit way.
Without loss of generality, up to suitable translation, rotation, reflection, and a change of variable, we assume that
x=(0,-d)
with
d>0
,
s_{x}=0
,
X(s_{x})=(0,0)
, and
X^{\prime}(s_{x})=(c,0)
with
c\geq\lambda>0
.
In order to bound
|V_{3}|
, it suffices to estimate

\displaystyle\;\left|X^{\prime\prime}_{j}(0)\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(cs^{\prime},d)\cdot\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\left|X^{\prime\prime}_{j}(0)\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(cs^{\prime},d)\mathds{1}_{\{\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime})\geq z\}}\,ds^{\prime}\,dz\right|.

By the assumptions on
\chi_{\delta}
, for any
z\in[0,1]
, the set
\{s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}:\,\chi_{\delta}(s^{\prime})\geq z\}
is an interval of the form
[-a,a]
for some
a\in[0,\pi]
.
We calculate that, with
x:=(cs^{\prime},d)
, for an arbitrary
a\in[0,\pi]
,

\int_{-a}^{a}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(cs^{\prime},d)\,ds^{\prime}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{-a}^{a}-\frac{x_{k}}{|x|^{2}}\delta_{ij}+\frac{\delta_{ik}x_{j}+x_{i}\delta_{jk}}{|x|^{2}}-\frac{2x_{i}x_{j}x_{k}}{|x|^{4}}\,ds^{\prime}.

Thanks to the oddness of the integral,

\int_{-a}^{a}\frac{x_{1}}{|x|^{2}}\,ds^{\prime}=\int_{-a}^{a}\frac{cs^{\prime}}{(cs^{\prime})^{2}+d^{2}}\,ds^{\prime}=0,\quad\mbox{and}\quad\int_{-a}^{a}\frac{x_{1}^{3}}{|x|^{4}}\,ds^{\prime}=0.

Hence, what remains in the integral must contain a factor of
x_{2}
in the numerator, which gives

\left|\int_{-a}^{a}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(cs^{\prime},d)\,ds^{\prime}\right|\leq C\int_{-a}^{a}\frac{x_{2}}{|x|^{2}}\,ds^{\prime}=C\int_{-a}^{a}\frac{d}{(cs^{\prime})^{2}+d^{2}}\,ds^{\prime}\leq Cc^{-1}\leq C\lambda^{-1}.

Therefore,
|V_{3}|\leq C\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}
.

Summarizing all these estimates yields that

|\nabla u(x)|\leq C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}\delta^{\alpha}+C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2}\delta^{-1}+C\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}},

where
C
depends on
\alpha
.
Observe that
\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\leq C_{\alpha}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}
for some
C_{\alpha}>0
that only depends on
\alpha
, so we take

\delta:=\left(\frac{\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}}{C_{\alpha}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}\in(0,1].

This gives

|\nabla u(x)|\leq C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{1+\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}+C\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\leq C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{1+\frac{\alpha}{1+\alpha}}\|X^{\prime\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}},

where
C
depends on
\alpha
.
In the last inequality, we used the interpolation inequality.
Therefore, the desired Lipschitz estimate for
u_{X}
follows.
∎

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
and
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, which satisfy
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
Let
(u,X)
be the unique local mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,T]
.
Let
M
,
\lambda
, and
Q
be defined as in (
5.1
)-(
5.3
), respectively.

(1)

For any
\beta\in[\frac{2}{p},1]
,

(5.28)

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

Note that the constant
C
depends on
T,\,p,\,\nu,\,\lambda,\,M,\,Q
, but not on
\beta
.

(2)

For any
\beta\in(0,1)
,

(5.29)

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{1+\beta}\|X^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T)}\eta^{1+\beta}\|\partial_{t}X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(T,\beta,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

(3)

For any
\beta\in[0,1)
,
\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}t^{1+\beta}\|X^{\prime\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}=0
.

(4)

For any
\beta\in[\frac{2}{p},1)
,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\|u(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,\beta,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q).

Moreover, for any
t\in(0,T]
,

(5.30)

\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t^{-1}.

Proof.

The first estimate follows by interpolating Lemma
5.4
(with
\gamma=\frac{2}{p}
there) and Lemma
5.9
(with
\gamma=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}
there).
The second estimate follows from Lemma
5.9
.
The third claim follows by interpolating this with Lemma
5.6
.
The
C^{\beta}_{x}
-estimate for
u
was proved in Lemma
5.4
.
It remains to show (
5.30
).

Fix
t_{0}\in(0,T]
.
To bound
\|\nabla u(t_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
, we follow the argument in the proof of Lemma
5.9
by resetting the initial time to be
t_{0}/2
.
Then (
5.23
) holds, which gives

\displaystyle\|\nabla u(t_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}-e^{\frac{\nu t_{0}}{2}\Delta}\right)\nabla u_{X(t_{0})}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\big\|\nabla\tilde{h}_{X,t_{0}/2}^{\nu}(t_{0})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\;+\Big\|e^{\frac{\nu t_{0}}{2}\Delta}u(t_{0}/2)\Big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}}+\big\|\tilde{B}_{\nu,t_{0}/2}[u](t_{0})\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}}.

By Lemma
5.7
with e.g.
\gamma=1-\frac{1}{p}
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|\nabla\tilde{h}_{X,t_{0}/2}^{\nu}(t_{0})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t_{0})^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}\left[\nu^{-\gamma}\lambda^{-1}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t_{0}]}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}}^{1-\gamma}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t_{0}]}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}^{\gamma}\right.

\displaystyle\;\qquad+C(\nu t_{0})^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\lambda^{-1-\gamma}t_{0}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\;\left.\quad\qquad\cdot\left(\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t_{0}]}\|X^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t_{0}]}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t_{0}]}\|\partial_{t}X(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)\left(t_{0}^{-\gamma}+t_{0}^{-\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\cdot t_{0}\cdot t_{0}^{-\gamma-\frac{1}{p}}\right)t_{0}^{\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2p}}.

Now by applying Lemma
5.8
(with
\beta=\frac{2}{p}
there), Lemma
5.10
(with
\alpha=\frac{1}{p}
there), and Lemma
A.1
, and also using the updated bounds in (
5.28
) and (
5.29
) to derive that (cf. the estimates in the proof of Lemma
5.9
)

\displaystyle\;\|\nabla u(t_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}(t_{0})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{\frac{p+2}{p+1}}\|X^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/p}}^{\frac{p}{p+1}}+C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q+C(\nu t_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-1}\sup_{\eta\in[t_{0}/2,t_{0}]}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|u(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{2/p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-1}\cdot t_{0}^{-\frac{1}{p}}C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)\cdot t_{0}^{-\frac{2}{p}}C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t_{0}^{-1}.

Since
t_{0}\in(0,T]
is arbitrary, this completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

The results in Proposition
5.4
are optimal in two ways.

•

The quantitative bounds for the higher-order norms of
X
and
u
have time-singularities at
t=0
with the optimal powers of
t
.
Indeed, the corresponding norms of the linear term
e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}
in (
2.19
) satisfies estimates with the very powers of
t
, and that further makes the estimates for
u
admit singularity in
t
as is claimed (see (
2.18
) and Lemma
3.2
).

•

In general, we do not expect the solution to enjoy even higher regularity than what is claimed here.
As is mentioned in Remark
2.2
, in general, the flow field
u
can at most be Lipschitz in space.
Then in view of Lemma
3.8
and its proof, the spatial regularity of
B_{\nu}[u]
cannot go beyond
C^{1,1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
By Lemma
A.4
, we can only expect
\mathcal{I}[B_{\nu}[u]\circ X]
to have spatial regularity no higher than
C^{2,1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})
, and by (
2.19
), that sets a limit for the regularity of
X
.

Before ending this subsection, let us prove a well-expected result on the volume conservation.

Lemma 5.11
.

For all
t\in[0,T]
,

\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}X(s,t)\times X^{\prime}(s,t)\,ds\equiv\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}X_{0}(s)\times X_{0}^{\prime}(s)\,ds,

i.e., the area of the region enclosed by the curve
X(\mathbb{T},t)
is constant in time.

Proof.

Let
\Omega_{t}
denote the region enclosed by the curve
\Gamma_{t}:=X(\mathbb{T},t)
.
Thanks to Proposition
5.4
, for any
t\in(0,T)
,
\Gamma_{t}
is a simple
C^{2,\beta}
-curve with
\beta\in(0,1)
.
Whenever
x\in\Gamma_{t}
, we shall use
n(x,t)
to denote the outer unit normal vector along
\Gamma_{t}
with respect to
\Omega_{t}
.

Thanks to the regularity of
X
and
\partial_{t}X
established in Proposition
5.4
, for
t\in(0,T)
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}X(s,t)\times X^{\prime}(s,t)\,ds=

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\partial_{t}X(s,t)\times X^{\prime}(s,t)+X(s,t)\times\partial_{t}X^{\prime}(s,t)\,ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}\partial_{t}X(s,t)\times X^{\prime}(s,t)\,ds.

We used the integration by parts in the last line.
Recall that
X
satisfies (
2.24
) in
(0,T)
(see Lemma
5.3
).
Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}X(s,t)\times X^{\prime}(s,t)\,ds=

\displaystyle\;-\int_{\mathbb{T}}u(X(s,t),t)\cdot X^{\prime}(s,t)^{\perp}\,ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}u(X(s,t),t)\cdot n(X(s,t),t)|X^{\prime}(s,t)|\,ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\Gamma_{t}}u(x,t)\cdot n(x,t)\,d\sigma=\int_{\Omega_{t}}\mathrm{div}\,u(x,t)\,dx=0.

In the last line, we used the divergence theorem, the divergence-free property of
u
, and the regularity of
u
proved in Proposition
5.4
.
Finally, using the continuity of
t\mapsto X(s,t)
in
C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
at
t=0
and
t=T
, we can conclude the desired claim.
∎

6.
The Zero-Reynolds-number Limit

We have constructed the mild solutions to the 2-D immersed boundary problem with the Navier-Stokes equation (
1.7
)-(
1.11
).
It is heuristically conceivable that, as
\nu\to+\infty
, or equivalently as
Re\to 0^{+}
, the solution would converge in a suitable sense to the one to the Stokes case (
1.12
)-(
1.16
), which formally corresponds to
Re=0
.
In this section, we shall rigorously justify this convergence and quantify the difference between these two cases.
The main result of this section is Proposition
6.2
.

As is reviewed in Section
1.3
, there have been quite a few results in the literature on the existence and uniqueness of solutions to the Stokes case (
1.12
)-(
1.16
), but here we choose to state the following one that is more compatible with our analysis.
Its proof is essentially the same as (and yet much simpler than) that for the Navier-Stokes case in Section
4
and Section
5
, so we shall omit it completely.
Readers are also referred to
[
8
, Theorem 1.12 and § 3.7]
for a similar result on the case of general nonlinear elasticity laws.

Proposition 6.1
.

Suppose that
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
satisfies
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
Then for some
T>0
that only depends on
X_{0}
, there exists a unique
X_{\dagger}=X_{\dagger}(s,t)
satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

X_{\dagger}\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\mathbb{T}))
, and
\inf_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{\dagger}(t)|_{*}>0
;

(ii)

for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}<+\infty;

(iii)

and, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

X_{\dagger}(t)=e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}+\mathcal{I}[g_{X_{\dagger}}](t),

where
g_{X_{\dagger}}
was defined as in (
2.11
) in terms of
X_{\dagger}
, and where
\mathcal{I}[\cdot]
was defined in (
2.21
).

Let
M_{0}:=\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}
and
\lambda_{0}:=|X_{0}|_{*}
.
The solution
X_{\dagger}
additionally enjoys the following properties.

(1)

|X_{\dagger}(t)|_{*}\geq\lambda_{0}/2
and
\|X^{\prime}_{\dagger}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\leq CM_{0}
for
t\in[0,T]
, where
C
is a universal constant.

(2)

X_{\dagger}\in C^{1}_{loc}((0,T]\times\mathbb{T})
, and the equation

\partial_{t}X_{\dagger}=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda X_{\dagger}+g_{X_{\dagger}}

holds pointwise in
(0,T]\times\mathbb{T}
.

(3)

For any
\beta\in(0,1]
,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\|\partial_{t}X_{\dagger}(\eta)\|_{C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(\lambda_{0},M_{0}).

(4)

For any
\beta\in(0,1)
,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\beta}\|\partial_{t}X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(\beta,\lambda_{0},M_{0}).

(5)

Given
\beta\in(0,1)
, let
\rho=\rho_{X_{0},\beta}(t)
be defined in terms of
X_{0}
as in (
4.3
).
Then there exists
T^{\prime}\in(0,T]
depending on
\beta
and
X_{0}
, such that for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
,

(6.1)

t^{\beta}\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\big(\rho(t)+t^{\beta}\big).

Remark 6.1
.

We shall call any function
X=X(s,t)
that satisfies the conditions
(i)
-
(iii)
to be a (mild) solution on
[0,T]
to the Stokes case of the 2-D immersed boundary problem (
1.12
)-(
1.16
).
Note that in the Stokes case, the flow field
u
can be recovered by
u(x,t)=u_{X(t)}(x)
.

Throughout this section, we assume
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with some
p\in(2,\infty)
and
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, and they satisfy that
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
We still define
M_{0}
,
\lambda_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
as in (
4.1
).

Let
X_{\dagger}=X_{\dagger}(s,t)
be the unique mild solution on
[0,T_{\dagger}]
to the Stokes case (
1.12
)-(
1.16
) constructed in Proposition
6.1
, which satisfies all the characterizations there.
In particular, it holds for
t\in[0,T_{\dagger}]
that

(6.2)

X_{\dagger}(t)=e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}+\mathcal{I}[g_{X_{\dagger}}](t).

Fix an arbitrary
\gamma\in[\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p},1)\cap(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
.
Note that this new range of
\gamma
may be more restrictive than the previously used one (cf. (
4.2
)); we will explain this in Remark
6.3
.
With this
\gamma
and with
\epsilon\leq M_{0}
to be chosen later (see (
6.22
)), by the time-continuity of
X_{\dagger}
and (
6.1
), we may further take
T_{\dagger}\leq 1
to be smaller if needed, so that

(6.3)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\dagger}]}\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 2M_{0},\quad\sup_{t\in(0,T_{\dagger}]}t^{\gamma}\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\epsilon,\quad\inf_{t\in[0,T_{\dagger}]}|X_{\dagger}(t)|_{*}\geq\frac{3\lambda_{0}}{4}.

and for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T_{\dagger}]
,

(6.4)

\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{1})-X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{8}.

Such
T_{\dagger}
will depend on
X_{0}
,
\gamma
, and
\epsilon
.

Next we introduce the setup in the Navier-Stokes case.
Without loss of generality, we assume
\nu\geq 1
in the rest of this section.
Let
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
denote the unique maximal mild solution to the Navier-Stokes case (
1.7
)-(
1.11
), with its maximal lifespan being
T_{\nu,*}\in(0,+\infty]
(see Definition
2.2
and Theorem
2.1
).
It holds on
[0,T_{\nu,*})
that (cf. (
2.18
) and (
2.19
))

(6.5)

\displaystyle u_{\nu}(t)=

\displaystyle\;u_{X_{\nu}(t)}+\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu}](t)=u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(t)+e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}+B_{\nu}[u_{\nu}](t),

(6.6)

\displaystyle X_{\nu}(t)=

\displaystyle\;e^{-\frac{t}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}+\mathcal{I}\big[g_{X_{\nu}}+\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu}]\circ X_{\nu}\big](t),

where (cf. (
2.20
))

\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu}](t)=\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu};\nu,u_{0}](t):=h_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}+e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{\nu}(t)})+B_{\nu}[u_{\nu}].

Let
\gamma
be taken as above.
By uniqueness, in short time,
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
satisfies all the characterizations in Proposition
4.1
(with the chosen
\gamma
).
Besides, on any compact sub-interval of
[0,T_{\nu,*})
,
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
satisfies all the properties established in Section
5
.
We thus define
T_{\nu}\in(0,T_{\nu,*})
to be the maximum possible time such that the following holds:

•

T_{\nu}\leq T_{\dagger}\leq 1
;

•

with the same
\epsilon
as in (
6.3
),

(6.7)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\nu}]}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 3M_{0},\quad\sup_{t\in(0,T_{\nu}]}t^{\gamma}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 2\epsilon,\quad\inf_{t\in[0,T_{\nu}]}|X_{\nu}(t)|_{*}\geq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{2},

•

for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T_{\nu}]
,

(6.8)

\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{1})-X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{\lambda_{0}}{4}.

•

and, with
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
to be chosen below,

(6.9)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\nu}]}\|u_{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq 2Q_{0}+\epsilon^{\prime}.

Here the maximality of
T_{\nu}
means that (
6.7
)-(
6.9
) hold on
[0,T_{\nu}]
, but if
T_{\nu}
gets replaced by any
T\in(T_{\nu},T_{\dagger}]
, at least one of the properties would fail.
Such
T_{\nu}
is well-defined due to the time-continuity of
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
(see Proposition
4.1
, Lemma
5.2
, and Lemma
5.4
) as well as Corollary
5.3
; we only point out that, by Corollary
5.3
, as
T\to T_{\nu,*}^{-}
,

\sup_{t\in(0,T]}t^{\gamma}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\left(\inf_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{\nu}(t)|_{*}\right)^{-1}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|u_{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

should diverge.

We choose the parameter
\epsilon^{\prime}
in (
6.9
) as follows.
By Lemma
3.2
,

\displaystyle\|u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{0}^{\prime}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2},

where
C_{0}^{\prime}
is universal constant depending only on
p
.
Then we let

(6.10)

\epsilon^{\prime}:=2C_{0}^{\prime}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}.

It will be clear in the proof of Lemma
6.2
below why this choice of
\epsilon^{\prime}
suffices.

The main result to prove in this section is as follows.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
p
,
(u_{0},X_{0})
,
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
, and
X_{\dagger}
be given as above.
Fix
\gamma\in[\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p},1)\cap(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
.
Define
M_{0}
,
\lambda_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
as in (
4.1
), let
T_{\dagger}
and
T_{\nu}
be defined as above (also see Remark
6.4
below).
Then there exists
\nu_{*}\geq 1
, which depends on
\gamma
,
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
, such that
T_{\nu}=T_{\dagger}
for any
\nu\geq\nu_{*}
.
Hence, for
\nu\geq\nu_{*}
, (
6.7
)-(
6.9
) hold on
[0,T_{\dagger}]
.
Moreover, for any
t\in(0,T_{\dagger}]
,

(6.11)

\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq Ct\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}},

(6.12)

\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq Ct\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}},

and

\|u_{\nu}(t)-u_{X_{\dagger}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\ln\nu\cdot t\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t),X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t))\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{s}}\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}},

where the constants
C
depend on
\gamma
,
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
.

Remark 6.2
.

For
\gamma\not\in[\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p},1)\cap(1-\frac{2}{p},1)
, similar estimates can be derived from Proposition
6.2
by the interpolation inequality.

To prove this proposition, let us first justify more uniform-in-
\nu
estimates for
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
based on (
6.7
) and (
6.9
).

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume (
6.7
) and (
6.9
).
When
\nu\geq 1
is taken to be suitably large, which depends on
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
, it holds that

(6.13)

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\nu^{\frac{1}{p}}\lambda_{0}^{-1}M_{0}^{2}+C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0}),

where
C
is a constant depending only on
p
, and

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0}),\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0}).

Proof.

By (
6.5
), Lemma
3.2
, and (
3.9
) in Lemma
3.8
(cf. (
4.20
)),

\displaystyle\;(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(t)-u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;CQ_{0}+C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\cdot\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;CQ_{0}

\displaystyle\;+C_{1}\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left(\nu^{\frac{1}{p}}\lambda_{0}^{-1}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T_{\nu}}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}\eta^{1/p}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/p}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)-u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\right),

where
C
and
C_{1}
only depend on
p
.
Note that, given the bounds for
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
from Proposition
4.1
,
\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)-u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}
is known to be finite.
In view of (
6.9
) and
T_{\nu}\leq T_{\dagger}\leq 1
, we may assume
\nu\geq 1
to be sufficiently large, which depends on
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
, so that

C_{1}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\nu T_{\nu})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Then with (
6.7
) and the interpolation inequality, we can obtain that

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)-u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq CQ_{0}+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\lambda_{0}^{-1}M_{0}^{2}\leq C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0}).

Here we used the fact that
\epsilon\leq M_{0}
.
As a result, by Lemma
3.2
,

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\nu^{\frac{1}{p}}\lambda_{0}^{-1}M_{0}^{2}+C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0}),

where
C
depends on
p
, and by (
5.7
),

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0}).

Similarly, by (
3.10
) in Lemma
3.8
, for any
t\in(0,T_{\nu}]
,

(6.14)

\begin{split}&\;\|B_{\nu}[u_{\nu}](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}}\\
\leq&\;C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-(1+\gamma)/2}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{(1+\gamma)/2}\|(u_{\nu}\otimes u_{\nu})(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{2/(1-\gamma)}}\\
\leq&\;C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\right)^{\frac{p(1-\gamma)}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\right)^{2-\frac{p(1-\gamma)}{2}},\end{split}

where
C
depends on
\gamma
.
Here we used the interpolation inequality and the fact
\frac{2}{1-\gamma}>p
.
Using (
6.9
) and (
6.13
),

\displaystyle(\nu t)^{\gamma}\|u_{\nu}(t)-u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}}\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q_{0}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{\gamma}\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})^{\frac{p(1-\gamma)}{2}}

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left(C\nu^{\frac{1}{p}}\lambda_{0}^{-1}M_{0}^{2}+C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\right)^{2-\frac{p(1-\gamma)}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q_{0}+C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\nu^{-1+\gamma}t^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}.

Since
\gamma\geq\frac{2}{p}
,
\nu\geq 1
, and
t\leq T_{\nu}\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}(\nu\eta)^{\gamma}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}(\nu\eta)^{\gamma}\|u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}}+C\nu^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q_{0}+C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu^{\gamma}\lambda_{0}^{-1-\gamma}M_{0}^{2}+C\nu^{\frac{\gamma}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}Q_{0}+C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0}),

where
C
depends on
\gamma
.
Hence, by (
5.7
),

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu}]}\eta^{\gamma}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{\gamma}\leq C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0}).

∎

We will use a continuity method to prove Proposition
6.2
.
The following lemma plays a crucial role.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
T_{\nu}\leq T_{\dagger}\leq 1
be defined as above, and assume (
6.3
), (
6.4
), and
(
6.7
)-(
6.9
), where
\epsilon
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
are chosen in (
6.22
) and (
6.10
), respectively.

When
\nu\geq 1
is sufficiently large, which depends on
\gamma
,
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
, it holds that

•

(6.15)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\nu}]}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{C_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{5}{2}M_{0},\quad\sup_{t\in(0,T_{\nu}]}t^{\gamma}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{3}{2}\epsilon,\quad\inf_{t\in[0,T_{\nu}]}|X_{\nu}(t)|_{*}\geq\frac{5\lambda_{0}}{8},

•

for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T_{\nu}]
,

(6.16)

\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{1})-X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{3\lambda_{0}}{16}.

•

and

(6.17)

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\nu}]}\|u_{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\frac{3}{4}\big(2Q_{0}+\epsilon^{\prime}\big).

Proof.

By Lemma
3.2
, (
3.8
) in Lemma
3.8
, (
6.5
), and (
6.7
), for any
t\in[0,T_{\nu}]
(cf. (
4.19
)),

\displaystyle\|u_{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\leq

\displaystyle\;\|u_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}+\|e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{p}}+\|B_{\nu}[u_{\nu}](t)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{0}^{\prime}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}+Q_{0}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\right)^{2},

where
C_{0}^{\prime}
and
C
are universal constants depending only on
p
.
By (
6.9
) and (
6.10
),

\|u_{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\leq C_{0}^{\prime}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}+Q_{0}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+C_{0}^{\prime}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)^{2}.

Since
T_{\nu}\leq T_{\dagger}\leq 1
, by taking
\nu
to be sufficiently large, which depends on
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
,
we can achieve that

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\nu}]}\|u_{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(Q_{0}+C_{0}^{\prime}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)=\frac{3}{4}\big(2Q_{0}+\epsilon^{\prime}\big),

which is (
6.17
).

By (
6.2
) and (
6.6
),

(6.18)

(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})(t)=\mathcal{I}\big[g_{X_{\nu}}-g_{X_{\dagger}}+\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu}]\circ X_{\nu}\big](t).

To bound
(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})
, we apply Lemma
A.4
as in (
4.32
) and find that, for any
t\in[0,T_{\nu}]
with
T_{\nu}\leq 1
,

(6.19)

\begin{split}&\;\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|(g_{X_{\nu}}-g_{X_{\dagger}})(\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\big(h_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}+e^{\nu\eta\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{\nu}(\eta)})\big)\circ X_{\nu}(\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}\\
&\;+C\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|B_{\nu}[u_{\nu}]\circ X_{\nu}(\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})},\end{split}

where
C
depends on
p
and
\gamma
.

By Lemma
3.11
and the interpolation inequality,

\displaystyle\;\|g_{X_{\nu}}(t)-g_{X_{\dagger}}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda_{0}^{-2}\big(\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\big)\big(\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\cdot\Big[\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)-X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\lambda_{0}^{-1}\big(\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}\big)\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)-X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{-\gamma}\lambda_{0}^{-2}M_{0}^{3/2}\left[\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\left(\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}+\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left[\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma/2}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime}-X_{\dagger}^{\prime})(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma/2}}+\lambda_{0}^{-1}M_{0}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|({X_{\nu}}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right].

By the interpolation inequality, (
6.3
), and (
6.7
), it simplifies to

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|(g_{X_{\nu}}-g_{X_{\dagger}})(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda_{0}^{-3}M_{0}^{5/2}\epsilon^{1/2}\left[\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|({X_{\nu}}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime}-X_{\dagger}^{\prime})(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right],

where the constant
C
depends only on
\gamma
.

The last two lines of (
6.19
) can be bounded as in (
4.33
) and (
6.14
), respectively.
Indeed, with
\gamma\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}
and

N_{\nu}:=\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu})}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T_{\nu})}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},

we proceed as in (
4.33
) to find that

(6.20)

\begin{split}&\;t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\big\|\big(h_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}+e^{\nu\eta\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{\nu}(\eta)})\big)\circ X_{\nu}(\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;Ct^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\left(Q_{0}+\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\right)M_{0}\\
&\;+Ct^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}\left(M_{0}^{2-\gamma}N_{\nu}^{\gamma}+\lambda_{0}^{-1}M_{0}^{2}N_{\nu}\cdot t^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)M_{0}\\
\leq&\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\cdot t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}},\end{split}

Here we used the bound
N_{\nu}\leq C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})
due to Lemma
6.1
.
On the other hand, following (
6.14
) and applying (
6.9
), (
6.10
), and Lemma
6.1
,

\displaystyle\;\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\big\|B_{\nu}[u_{\nu}]\circ X_{\nu}(\eta)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{\gamma}\cdot\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\right)^{\frac{p(1-\gamma)}{2}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\right)^{2-\frac{p(1-\gamma)}{2}}M_{0}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\cdot t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}.

In the last line, we used the assumptions that
p>2
,
\gamma>\frac{2}{p}
,
\nu\geq 1
, and
t\leq 1
.

Combining all these estimates into (
6.19
), we obtain that

(6.21)

\begin{split}&\;\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C_{2}\lambda_{0}^{-3}M_{0}^{5/2}\epsilon^{1/2}\left[\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|({X_{\nu}}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime}-X_{\dagger}^{\prime})(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right]\\
&\;+C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\cdot t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}},\end{split}

where
C_{2}
depends only on
\gamma
.
