In view of this, we shall choose
\epsilon\leq M_{0}
suitably small, so that

(6.22)

C_{2}\lambda_{0}^{-3}M_{0}^{5/2}\epsilon^{1/2}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Then with
T_{\nu}\leq T_{\dagger}\leq 1
, for any
t\in(0,T_{\nu}]
,

(6.23)

\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq C_{3}(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\cdot t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}},

We additionally require
\nu
to be sufficiently small, which depends on
\gamma
,
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
, such that

C_{3}(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\cdot\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\leq\min\left(\frac{M_{0}}{2},\,\frac{\epsilon}{2},\,\frac{\lambda_{0}}{32}\right).

Then from (
6.3
) and (
6.4
), one can readily show that
X_{\nu}
satisfies (
6.15
) and (
6.16
).

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

As is explained in Remark
2.4
, the estimates (
6.21
) and (
6.23
) are optimal in terms of the
\nu
- and
t
-dependence given the presence of the term
e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}
in the dynamics of
X_{\nu}
.
The assumption
\gamma\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}
was made to achieve such optimal form of the bounds.
We point out that it was used when estimating
\|h_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}\circ X_{\nu}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}
in (
6.20
); also see (
4.26
) in Lemma
4.2
.

Remark 6.4
.

Combining (
6.3
), (
6.4
), and (
6.22
), we can conclude that
T_{\dagger}
depends on
\gamma
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
X_{0}
.

Then the convergence as
\nu\to+\infty
follows immediately.

Proof of Proposition
6.2
.

We may define
\nu_{*}
to be sufficiently large so that, as long as
\nu\geq\nu_{*}
, Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.2
hold.
Such
\nu_{*}
depends on
\gamma
,
p
,
\lambda_{0}
,
M_{0}
, and
Q_{0}
.
In the rest of the proof, we always assume
\nu\geq\nu_{*}
.

The claim that
T_{\nu}=T_{\dagger}
for all
\nu\geq\nu_{*}
follows from Lemma
6.2
and the time-continuity of
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
.
Indeed, if
T_{\nu}<T_{\dagger}
for some
\nu\geq\nu_{*}
, then the time-continuity of
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
as well as (
6.15
)-(
6.17
) implies that there exists
T_{\nu}^{\prime}\in(T_{\nu},T_{\dagger}]
, such that (
6.7
)-(
6.9
) still hold with
T_{\nu}
there replaced by
T_{\nu}^{\prime}
.
This contradicts with the maximality of
T_{\nu}
.

The estimates (
6.7
)-(
6.9
) for
(u_{\nu},X_{\nu})
on
[0,T_{\dagger}]
then follow.
The bound (
6.12
) has been proved in (
6.23
).

To show (
6.11
), we derive by Lemma
3.1
, Lemma
3.6
(also see the arguments in Lemma
4.2
), (
3.9
) in Lemma
3.8
, Lemma
A.1
that (also see (
4.27
)), for all
t\in(0,T_{\nu}]
,

\displaystyle\;\|\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu}](t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\|h_{X_{\nu}}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\big\|e^{\nu t\Delta}(u_{0}-u_{X_{\nu}(t)})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|B_{\nu}[u_{\nu}](t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\min\left(\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\;\cdot\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)^{1-\gamma}\left(\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\gamma}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right)^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\min\left(\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}},\,(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p}}\right)t^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\lambda_{0}^{-1}\left(\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(\eta)\|_{C_{s}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right.

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\left.+\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\eta^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\|\partial_{t}X_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}}\right)

\displaystyle\;+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{0}-u_{X_{\nu}(t)}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}

\displaystyle\;+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u_{\nu}(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}.

By Lemma
6.1
as well as (
6.7
) and (
6.9
), for any
0<t\leq T_{\dagger}\leq 1
,

(6.24)

\begin{split}&\;\|\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu}](t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\leq&\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}+C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\big(Q_{0}+\lambda_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}\big)\\
&\;+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2}{p}}C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\left[C(p)\nu^{\frac{1}{p}}\lambda_{0}^{-1}M_{0}^{2}+C(p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\right]\\
\leq&\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}.\end{split}

Moreover, by Lemma
3.11
, (
6.3
), (
6.7
), (
6.12
), and the interpolation inequality,

\displaystyle\;\|g_{X_{\nu}}(t)-g_{X_{\dagger}}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda_{0}^{-2}\big(\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\cdot\Big[\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)-X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/2}}+\lambda_{0}^{-1}\big(\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/2}}+\|X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/2}}\big)\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)-X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda_{0}^{-2}M_{0}^{2}\Big[t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot t^{\frac{1}{2}}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)-X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/2}}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{0}^{-1}M_{0}\|X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t)-X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda_{0}^{-3}M_{0}^{3}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\cdot t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}.

By (
6.18
),

\displaystyle\|X_{\nu}(t)-X_{\dagger}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\int_{0}^{t}\|g_{X_{\nu}}(\tau)-g_{X_{\dagger}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}+\|\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu}](\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\cdot t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}.

Finally, by Lemma
3.1
, Lemma
3.17
, and (
3.19
), with
\delta\in(0,t]
to be chosen,

\displaystyle\;\|u_{X_{\nu}(t)}-u_{X_{\dagger}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\int_{0}^{\infty}\big\|\Delta e^{\tau\Delta}\big(u_{X_{\nu}(t)}-u_{X_{\dagger}(t)}\big)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{0}^{\delta}\lambda_{0}^{-1-\frac{2}{p}}\tau^{-1+\frac{1}{p}}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{2/p}}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\;+C\int_{\delta}^{t}\lambda_{0}^{-2}\tau^{-1}

\displaystyle\;\qquad\quad\cdot\left(\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{s}}\|X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}}\right)d\tau

\displaystyle\;+C\int_{t}^{\infty}\tau^{-3/2}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\lambda_{0}^{-1}\tau^{-3/2}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\delta^{1/p}\cdot\lambda_{0}^{-1-\frac{2}{p}}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{2/p}}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\;+C\lambda_{0}^{-2}\ln\big(\delta^{-1}t\big)

\displaystyle\;\quad\cdot\left(\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{s}}\|X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\;+Ct^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|(X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger})^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}+\lambda_{0}^{-1}\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}\|X_{\nu}-X_{\dagger}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right).

Using (
6.3
), (
6.7
), (
6.11
) and (
6.12
), we simplify it as

\displaystyle\;\|u_{X_{\nu}(t)}-u_{X_{\dagger}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\delta^{\frac{1}{p}}\cdot t^{-\frac{2}{p}}\lambda_{0}^{-1-\frac{2}{p}}M_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\;+C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\ln\big(\delta^{-1}t\big)\left(\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime},X_{\dagger}^{\prime})\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{s}}\cdot t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}+t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\;+C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\cdot t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}+t\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}\right).

Take
\delta:=t^{2}(\nu t)^{-1}\in(0,t]
.
Since
\nu\geq 1
and
t\leq T_{\dagger}\leq 1
, we find that

\displaystyle\|u_{X_{\nu}(t)}-u_{X_{\dagger}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\ln\nu\cdot t\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t),X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t))\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{s}}\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\;+C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}.

Therefore, by (
6.5
) and (
6.24
),

\displaystyle\|u_{\nu}(t)-u_{X_{\dagger}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\|u_{X_{\nu}(t)}-u_{X_{\dagger}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|\tilde{w}[u_{\nu},X_{\nu}](t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})\ln\nu\cdot t\|(X_{\nu}^{\prime}(t),X_{\dagger}^{\prime}(t))\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{s}}\cdot(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\;+C(\gamma,p,\lambda_{0},M_{0},Q_{0})(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{p}}.

∎

7.
Local Solutions with
u_{0}\in L^{2}\cap L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

Let
(u_{0},X_{0})
be assumed as in Section
5
.
From now on, let us additionally assume that
u_{0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
This is motivated by the need of establishing the energy law of the system (see Proposition
7.1
), which will be used in the study of the global solutions in Section
8
.

We still use
(u,X)
to denote the unique mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,T]
, and let
M
,
\lambda
,
Q
be defined as in (
5.1
)-(
5.3
), respectively.
Besides, we denote
K_{0}:=\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}<+\infty
.

Lemma 7.1
.

u\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, and

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q,K_{0}).

Proof.

We first derive an upper bound for
\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
.
This can be achieved by repeatedly using (
2.18
) and Lemma
3.8
.
We only sketch it.

For any
q\in[2,p]
, by (
2.18
), Lemma
3.2
, and (
3.6
) in Lemma
3.8
, with
r\in[1,q]
satisfying that
0<\frac{1}{r}<\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{q}
,

\displaystyle\;\|u(t)\|_{L_{x}^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\|u_{0}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}+C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2q}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{1}{q}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{r}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}\|(u\otimes u)(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{r}}.

If
p\in(2,4]
, we simply take
(q,r)=(2,\frac{p}{2})
to obtain that

\|u(t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{4}}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}M^{2}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{p}}^{2}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q,K_{0}).

If
p>4
, we first take
q=r=p/2
, and find that

\|u(t)\|_{L_{x}^{p/2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p/2}_{x}}+C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}M^{2}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{p}}^{2}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q,K_{0}).

Note that
\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p/2}_{x}}\leq C(K_{0},Q)
by interpolation.
Then we bootstrap with this updated estimate.
After finite steps of iteration, we can conclude that

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q,K_{0}).

Next we prove
u\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
. With
r:=(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p})^{-1}\in(1,2)
, we apply Lemma
3.2
and (
3.6
) in Lemma
3.8
to find that, for
t\in[0,T]
,

\big\|u_{X}^{\nu}(t)+B_{\nu}[u](t)\big\|_{L^{r}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2r}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{1}{r}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}^{2}+C\nu^{-1}(\nu t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{p}},

which is uniformly bounded for
t\in[0,T]
.
On the other hand,
t\mapsto u_{X}^{\nu}(t)+B_{\nu}[u](t)=u(t)-e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}
is continuous in the
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
-topology due to Lemma
5.2
and the fact
u_{0}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
By interpolation,
t\mapsto u_{X}^{\nu}(t)+B_{\nu}[u](t)
is also continuous in the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
-topology.
Since
u_{0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
implies that
t\mapsto e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}
is continuous in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we use (
2.18
) to conclude that
u\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))
.
∎

Let us also show that
\nabla u(t)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for all
t\in(0,T]
.

Lemma 7.2
.

\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{1/2}\|\nabla u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q,K_{0}).

In particular,
u(t)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for any
t\in(0,T]
.

Proof.

Recall (
2.18
).
By Lemma
A.1
,

\|\nabla e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=C(\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}K_{0},

where
C
is a universal constant.
To bound the
L^{2}
-norm of
\nabla u_{X}^{\nu}
, we interpolate the results of Lemma
3.1
to find that, with
\gamma\in(0,1)
to be chosen,

\displaystyle\;\|\nabla\Delta e^{t\Delta}u_{X}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\min\left(\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}t^{-\frac{5}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}},\,\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{5}{4}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{2}},\,t^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\right).

By (
2.13
), for any
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
, with
\delta\in(0,\frac{t}{2}]
to be determined,

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\nu\int_{t-\delta}^{t}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}(\nu(t-\tau))^{-\frac{5}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}\tau^{-\gamma}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\cdot\tau^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\;+C\nu\int_{t/2}^{t-\delta}\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\nu(t-\tau))^{-\frac{5}{4}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{s}^{2}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\;+C\nu\int_{0}^{t/2}(\nu(t-\tau))^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,\gamma,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)\left[\delta^{-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\gamma}{2}}t^{-\gamma}+\mathds{1}_{\{\delta<\frac{t}{2}\}}\delta^{-\frac{1}{4}}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right].

In the last inequality, we used Proposition
5.4
.
Letting
\gamma:=\frac{3}{4}
and
\delta:=\frac{1}{2}\min(t^{2},t)
, we find that

\|\nabla u_{X}^{\nu}(t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q)t^{-1/2}.

Finally, by (
2.15
), again with
\delta\in[0,\frac{t}{2}]
to be determined,

\displaystyle B_{\nu}[u](t)=e^{\nu\delta\Delta}B_{\nu}[u](t-\delta)-\int_{t-\delta}^{t}e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}\mathbb{P}(u\cdot\nabla u)(\tau)\,d\tau.

Hence, by (
3.7
) in Lemma
3.8
, Proposition
5.4
, and Lemma
A.1
,

\displaystyle\;\|\nabla B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\big\|\nabla e^{\nu\delta\Delta}B_{\nu}[u](t-\delta)\big\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\left\|\int_{t-\delta}^{t}e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}\nabla\mathbb{P}(u\cdot\nabla u)(\tau)\,d\tau\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|B_{\nu}[u](t-\delta)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+C\int_{t-\delta}^{t}(\nu(t-\tau))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|(u\cdot\nabla u)(\tau)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(\nu\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{-1}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\sup_{\eta\in(0,t]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{p}}+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\sup_{\eta\in[t/2,t]}\|\nabla u(\eta)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q,K_{0})\left[\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}}+\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{-1}\right].

In the last line, we used Proposition
5.4
and Lemma
7.1
.
Choosing
\delta:=\frac{1}{2}\min(t^{2-\frac{2}{p}},t)
yields that

\|\nabla B_{\nu}[u](t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C(T,p,\nu,\lambda,M,Q,K_{0})t^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Here in the case
t\geq 1
, we absorbed some powers of
t
into the constant
C
which may depend on
T
.

Combining all the estimates, we conclude the desired estimate.
∎

Now we can prove the energy law of the system.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{2}\cap L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
and
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, which satisfy
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
and
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
.
Let
(u,X)
be the unique local mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
) on
[0,T]
.
Then for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L_{s}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}+\nu^{-1}\|u(t)\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,d\tau=\|X^{\prime}_{0}\|_{L_{s}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}+\nu^{-1}\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

Proof.

Take
\varphi=\varphi(x)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
to be a non-negative radial mollifier in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, satisfying that
\varphi
is supported in the unit disc
B_{1}(0)
centered at the origin, and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi(x)\,dx=1
.
For
\varepsilon>0
, we define
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x):=\varepsilon^{-2}\varphi(x/\varepsilon)
.

Fix
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
.
Take
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset(0,T)
and consider an arbitrary
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
.
By Proposition
5.4
,
X=X(\cdot,t)\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})
for any fixed
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and
|X(t)|_{*}\geq\lambda
.
By (
2.7
), we claim that
\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u_{X(t)}
solves

(7.1)

-\Delta(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u_{X(t)})=\mathbb{P}f_{X,\varepsilon},\quad f_{X,\varepsilon}(x,t):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x-X(s,t))X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds,

where
\mathbb{P}
denotes the Leray projection operator in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Indeed, one can readily show that
f_{X,\varepsilon}(\cdot,t)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and has integral
0
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, so

\displaystyle(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathbb{P}f_{X,\varepsilon}=

\displaystyle\;G*f_{X,\varepsilon}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G(x-y)\int_{\mathbb{T}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(y-X(s,t))X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds\,dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)\int_{\mathbb{T}}G(y-X(s,t))X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds\,dy=\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u_{X(t)}.

Hence, by (
2.18
) as well as (
2.13
) and (
2.15
),

\displaystyle\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u(t)=

\displaystyle\;\varphi_{\varepsilon}*e^{\nu t\Delta}u_{0}-\nu\int_{0}^{t}e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}\Delta\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u_{X(\tau)}\,d\tau-\int_{0}^{t}e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\mathbb{P}(u\cdot\nabla u)(\tau)\,d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;e^{\nu t\Delta}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}\mathbb{P}\big[\nu f_{X,\varepsilon}-\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\cdot\nabla u)\big](\tau)\,d\tau.

By virtue of Proposition
5.4
, Lemma
7.1
, and (
7.1
), one can show that, on
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset(0,T)
, the mappings
t\mapsto f_{X,\varepsilon}(t)
and
t\mapsto\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\cdot\nabla u)(t)
are continuous in the
C^{\beta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
-topology for some
\beta\in(0,1)
; we omit the details.
This allows us to deduce that

\displaystyle\;(\partial_{t}-\nu\Delta)(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(\partial_{t}-\nu\Delta)e^{\nu t\Delta}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u_{0})+(\partial_{t}-\nu\Delta)\int_{0}^{t}e^{\nu(t-\tau)\Delta}\mathbb{P}\big[\nu f_{X,\varepsilon}-\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\cdot\nabla u)\big](\tau)\,d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big[\nu f_{X,\varepsilon}-\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\cdot\nabla u)\big](t).

We note that
\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u
is smooth in space.
Besides, also by Proposition
5.4
and Lemma
7.1
, the last line of the above equation is smooth in space and uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for all
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
.
It is thus legitimate to take inner product of the above equation with
\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u
, which gives

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\nu\|\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\partial_{t}-\nu\Delta)(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\cdot(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big[\nu f_{X,\varepsilon}-\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\cdot\nabla u)\big]\cdot(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\,dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\nu\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)(x)\int_{\mathbb{T}}\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x-X(s,t))X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds\,\,dx

\displaystyle\;-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big[\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\cdot\nabla u)-(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\cdot\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\big]\cdot(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\,dx.

Indeed, the first equality follows from
[
52
, Chapter III, Section 1.4, Lemma 1.2]
as well as the smoothness of
(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)
and its time derivative.
In the last equality, we used the fact that, since
\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u\in L^{2}\cap C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and it is divergence-free, it holds that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\cdot\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\cdot(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\,dx=0.

Hence,

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}+\nu\|\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\nu\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\varphi_{\varepsilon})*u\big](X(s,t))\cdot X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds

\displaystyle\;+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big[\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\otimes u)-(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\otimes(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\big]:\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\,dx.

Integrate this over
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
yields that

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}\left[\|(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)(t_{2})\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-\|(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)(t_{1})\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\right]+\nu\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\nu\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\varphi_{\varepsilon})*u\big](X(s,t))\cdot X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\;+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big[\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\otimes u)-(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\otimes(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\big]:\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\,dx\,dt.

Now send
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
.
Thanks to Proposition
5.4
, Lemma
7.1
, and Lemma
7.2
,
u(t)
is uniformly bounded in
H^{1}\cap W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
, so by the dominated convergence theorem,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\|(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)(t_{i})\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}=\|u(t_{i})\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\quad(i=1,2),

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,dt=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,dt,

and

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big[\varphi_{\varepsilon}*(u\otimes u)-(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\otimes(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\big]:\nabla(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*u)\,dx\,dt=0.

Moreover, by Lemma
5.3
and Proposition
5.4
,

\displaystyle\;\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\nu\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\big[(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\varphi_{\varepsilon})*u\big](X(s,t))\cdot X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\nu\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}u(X(s,t))\cdot X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds\,dt=\nu\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\partial_{t}X(s,t)\cdot X^{\prime\prime}(s,t)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;-\nu\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\partial_{t}X^{\prime}(s,t)\cdot X^{\prime}(s,t)\,ds\,dt=-\nu\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,t)\|_{L_{s}^{2}}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;-\frac{\nu}{2}\left[\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{2})\|_{L_{s}^{2}}^{2}-\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,t_{1})\|_{L_{s}^{2}}^{2}\right].

Combining these limits with the identity above, we obtain that

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}\left[\|u(t_{2})\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}-\|u(t_{1})\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\right]+\nu\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;-\frac{\nu}{2}\left[\|X^{\prime}(t_{2})\|_{L_{s}^{2}}^{2}-\|X^{\prime}(t_{1})\|_{L_{s}^{2}}^{2}\right].

Since
0<t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
are arbitrary, we send
t_{1}\to 0^{+}
and use the time-continuity of
(u,X)
in
L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times C_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})
to conclude the desired energy estimate.
∎

8.
Global Solutions for Small Initial Datum

In the section, we shall prove that starting from small initial datum, we can obtain global solutions (see Definition
2.2
).
By saying the initial data is small, we mean that it is close to an equilibrium (i.e., a steady-state solution) in a suitable sense, which will be specified later.
The main result of this section is Proposition
8.1
.

8.1.
Preliminaries

Recall that, for
X=X(s)
, we defined its Fourier expansion in (
2.4
) and (
2.5
).
It is not difficult to show that

(8.1)

\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}=2\pi\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}n^{2}|a_{n}|^{2}.

Also recall that, if
X=X(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
is a Jordan curve parameterized in the counterclockwise direction, its effective radius
R>0
was defined in (
2.3
).
Using the Fourier expansion (
2.4
) and (
2.5
) of
X=X(s)
, we have that

(8.2)

\pi R^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}X(s)\times X^{\prime}(s)\,ds=\pi\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}n|a_{n}|^{2}.

We claim that the only equilibria (i.e., steady-states) of the system (
1.7
)-(
1.10
) are

u(x)\equiv 0,\quad X(s)=b_{0}+\begin{pmatrix}\cos s&-\sin s\\
\sin s&\cos s\end{pmatrix}b_{1}

for some
b_{0},b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
with
|b_{1}|>0
. Indeed, in order for
(u,X)
to be a steady-state solution, the energy law in Proposition
7.1
and the decay condition of
u
at spatial infinity imply that
u\equiv 0
.
Then one can show that
p
is constant in the interior and exterior of the domain enclosed by the curve
X(\mathbb{T})
.
The rest of the argument is the same as that in
[
38
, § 5.1]
.

Motivated by this, for
X=X(s)
, we defined
X^{*}
and
\Pi X
in (
2.6
), where
X^{*}(s)
is closest equilibrium to
X(s)
in the
L^{2}
-sense, while
\Pi X
characterizes deviation of
X
from an equilibrium.
We list some simple facts regarding
\Pi X
without proofs.

•

The operator
\Pi
commutes with the differentiation, and it is bounded in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{T})
and
\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})
for every
\gamma\geq 0
, namely,

(8.3)

\|\Pi X\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|X\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},\quad\|\Pi X\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\|X\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}.

•

With
R
defined in terms of
X
by (
8.2
), it holds that

(8.4)

\frac{1}{2}\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\leq\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}-2\pi R^{2}\leq 2\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}.

Indeed,
n^{2}-n\in[\frac{1}{2}n^{2},2n^{2}]
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{1\}
while it vanishes for
n=1
, so (
8.4
) follows from (
8.1
) and (
8.2
).
This in particular implies
\|X^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{2}}^{2}\geq 2\pi R^{2}
, where
2\pi R^{2}
is the total elastic energy of a string configuration in equilibrium with the effective radius being
R
.

•

If
\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq\frac{1}{16}R
, then

(8.5)

\|{X^{*}}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq R+\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}},\quad\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq R+2\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}},

and

(8.6)

|X|_{*}\geq\frac{2}{\pi}R-2\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}.

Indeed, assuming the expansion (
2.4
), we find by (
8.1
) and (
8.2
) that

|R^{2}-|a_{1}|^{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{s}}^{2}\leq\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{2},

so under the assumption,

R-\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq|a_{1}|\leq R+\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}.

Then the desired estimates follow from the facts that

\|{X^{*}}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}=|a_{1}|,\quad\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\leq|a_{1}|+\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}},\quad|X|_{*}\geq\frac{2}{\pi}|a_{1}|-\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}.

8.2.
Proof of the solution being global

Following the conditions of Theorem
2.6
, we assume that
u_{0}=u_{0}(x)\in L^{2}\cap L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
for some
p\in(2,\infty)
and
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{T})
, which satisfies
\mathrm{div}\,u_{0}=0
,
|X_{0}|_{*}>0
, and
X_{0}
is parameterized in the counterclockwise direction.
In order to show that we can obtain a global solution when
(u_{0},X_{0})
is small in a suitable sense, we make some preparations.

(i)

Let
(u,X)
be the maximal mild solution to (
1.7
)-(
1.11
), with its maximal lifespan being
T_{*}\in(0,+\infty]
(see Definition
2.2
).

(ii)

Let
R
be defined by (
8.2
), with
X
there replaced by
X_{0}=X_{0}(s)
.
For the mild solution
(u,X)
, by Lemma
5.11
, the effective radius of
X(\cdot,t)
is time-invariant, so it is always
R
.

(iii)

Denote

(8.7)

\varepsilon_{0}^{2}:=2\|\Pi X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}+\nu^{-1}\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

By Theorem
2.5
and (
8.4
), for all
t\in[0,T_{*})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\Pi X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}+\nu^{-1}\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\;\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}-2\pi R^{2}+\nu^{-1}\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}-2\pi R^{2}+\nu^{-1}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;2\|\Pi X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L_{s}^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}+\nu^{-1}\|u_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}=\varepsilon_{0}^{2},

so for any
t\in[0,T_{*})
,

(8.8)

\|\Pi X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\sqrt{2}\varepsilon_{0},\quad\nu^{-1}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\nu^{-1/2}.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
p
,
(u_{0},X_{0})
,
(u,X)
, and
R
be given as above.
There exists
\varepsilon_{*}>0
, which depends on
p
,
\nu
, and
R
, such that as long as

(8.9)

e_{0}:=\|\Pi X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\varepsilon_{*},

the solution
(u,X)
is global.

Moreover, it holds for all
t>0
that

\|u(t)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq CR^{1+\frac{2}{p}},

where
C
depends on
p
,

\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq CR,\quad|X(t)|_{*}\geq cR,

where
C>c>0
are universal constants, and

\|X^{\prime}(t)\|_{\dot{C}^{1/p}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C(p,\nu,R)\big(1+t^{-1/p}\big).

Lastly,

\|\Pi X^{\prime}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}+\nu^{-1/2}\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\big(1+\nu^{-1/2}\big)e_{0},

where
C
is universal.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that

(8.10)

\|u_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq R^{1+\frac{2}{p}},

and, for some
\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{16})
to be determined,

(8.11)

\|\Pi X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\delta_{0}R.

By (
8.5
) and (
8.6
),

\|X_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq(1+2\delta_{0})R,\quad|X_{0}|_{*}\geq\left(\frac{2}{\pi}-2\delta_{0}\right)R.

By Theorem
2.1
, there exists
T>0
such that, the maximal lifespan of
(u,X)
satisfies
T_{*}>T
, and on
[0,T]
,

(8.12)

\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 2(1+2\delta_{0})R,\quad\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}\eta^{\frac{1}{p}}\|X^{\prime}(\cdot,\eta)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/p}(\mathbb{T})}\leq 4(1+2\delta_{0})R,

(8.13)

|X(t)|_{*}\geq\left(\frac{1}{\pi}-\delta_{0}\right)R,

and

(8.14)

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\sup_{\eta\in(0,T]}(\nu\eta)^{\frac{1}{p}}\|u(\cdot,\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq CR^{1+\frac{2}{p}},

where
C
only depends on
p
.

We claim that, if
\delta_{0}
in (
8.11
) is taken to be sufficiently small, which depends on
p
,
\nu
, and
R
, the above
T
can be made to only depend on
p
,
\nu
, and
R
as well (but not implicitly on
X_{0}
).
Indeed, with
\gamma:=\max(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p},1-\frac{1}{p})
, we define as in (
4.3
) and (
4.5
) that

\rho_{X_{0},\gamma}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{\prime}\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},\quad\phi_{X_{0}}(t):=\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\big\|X_{0}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}.

Using (
2.6
), (
8.5
), (
8.11
), and Lemma
A.2
, we derive that

(8.15)

\rho_{X_{0},\gamma}(t)\leq\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}{X_{0}^{*}}^{\prime}\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}+\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\gamma}\big\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}\Pi X_{0}^{\prime}\big\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}}\leq C\big(t^{\gamma}R+\delta_{0}R\big),

where
C
only depends on
p
, and

(8.16)

\phi_{X_{0}}(t)\leq\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}\big\|X_{0}^{*}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}X_{0}^{*}\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}}+\big\|\Pi X_{0}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}\Pi X_{0}\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}}\leq C\big(tR+\delta_{0}R\big),

where
C
is a universal constant.
By virtue of Remark
4.4
, (
8.5
), (
8.6
), and (
8.11
),
T
only needs to be sufficiently small, so that

(8.17)

\begin{split}&\mbox{$(\rho_{X_{0},\gamma}(T)+T^{\sigma})$ and $\phi_{X_{0}}(T)$ are smaller than}\\
&\mbox{some constants that depend on $p$, $\nu$, and $R$,}\end{split}

where
\sigma:=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p})
.
In view of (
8.15
) and (
8.16
), we can find a sufficiently small
\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{16})
, and then choose
T\in(0,1)
to be small as well, both of which only depend on
p
,
\nu
, and
R
, so that under the assumption (
8.11
), the smallness condition (
8.17
) is satisfied.
We shall fix this choice of
\delta_{0}
and
T
in the rest of the proof.

By the interpolation inequality, (
8.3
), (
8.8
), and (
8.12
),

\|\Pi X^{\prime}(T/2)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\|\Pi X^{\prime}(T/2)\|_{L_{s}^{2}}^{\frac{2}{2+p}}\|X^{\prime}(T/2)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1/p}}^{\frac{p}{2+p}}\leq C\varepsilon_{0}^{\frac{2}{2+p}}\big(T^{-\frac{1}{p}}R\big)^{\frac{p}{2+p}},

where the constant
C
only depends on
p
.
Similarly, by the interpolation inequality, (
8.8
), and (
8.14
),

\|u(T/2)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\|u(T/2)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2}{p}}\|u(T/2)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\leq C\big(\varepsilon_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)^{\frac{2}{p}}\left[(\nu T)^{-\frac{1}{p}}R^{1+\frac{2}{p}}\right]^{1-\frac{2}{p}},

where
C
only depends on
p
as well.
Since
\delta_{0}
and
T
only depend on
p
,
\nu
, and
R
, by assuming
\varepsilon_{0}
to be sufficiently small, which only depends on
p
,
\nu
, and
R
as well, we can make

\|u(T/2)\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq R^{1+\frac{2}{p}},\quad\|\Pi X^{\prime}(T/2)\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\delta_{0}R.

Now we view
(u(T/2),X(T/2))
as the new initial data, and apply Theorem
2.1
to obtain its maximal local solution, which we denote by
(\tilde{u},\tilde{X})
.
Since
(u(T/2),X(T/2))
enjoys the same bound as
(u_{0},X_{0})
, by the same argument as above, we find that for the same
T>0
,
(\tilde{u},\tilde{X})
is well-defined on
[0,T]
and enjoys the same estimates as in (
8.12
)-(
8.14
).
By uniqueness,
(\tilde{u},\tilde{X})
must coincide with the restriction of
(u,X)
on
[T/2,T_{*})
(up to a time shift by
T
).
Hence,
T_{*}>\frac{3}{2}T
.
One may repeat this infinitely to obtain
T_{*}=+\infty
, i.e.,
(u,X)
is a global solution.

We note that the smallness conditions (
8.10
) and (
8.11
) as well as the smallness of
\varepsilon_{0}
(see (
8.7
)) can be achieved by requiring
\varepsilon_{*}
in (
8.9
) to be sufficiently small, which only depends on
p
,
\nu
, and
R
.

Finally, the claimed estimates follow from the fact that (
8.8
) and (
8.12
)-(
8.14
) hold for
(u,X)
on each time interval
[\frac{1}{2}(k-1)T,\frac{1}{2}(k+1)T]
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
∎

Appendix A
Parabolic Estimates

We recall some standard parabolic estimates for the heat equation in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, whose proof will be omitted.

Lemma A.1
.

Recall that, with
\mu\in(0,1)
, the Morrey norm
M^{1,\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
was defined in (
2.1
).
Let
\{e^{t\Delta}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the standard heat semigroup on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Let
f=f(x)
be defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Assume
\gamma\in(0,1)
.

(1)

For any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\|e^{t\Delta}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{-\mu/2}\|f\|_{M^{1,\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{3}(-\Delta)^{-1}e^{t\Delta}f\|_{M^{1,\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{-1/2}\|f\|_{M^{1,\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
C
depends on
\mu
.

(2)

For any
\beta>0
and
t>0
,

\|\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}e^{t\Delta}f\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Ct^{-(\mu+\beta)/2}\|f\|_{M^{1,\mu}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
C
depends on
\beta
and
\mu
.

(3)

For any
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\beta>\gamma
, and any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}e^{t\Delta}f\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{-\beta/2}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1}e^{t\Delta}f\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{-(\beta-\gamma)/2}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

\displaystyle\|e^{t\Delta}f-f\|_{C(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq

\displaystyle\;Ct^{\gamma/2}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
C
depends on
\beta
and
\gamma
.

(4)

For any
\beta\geq 0
,
p\in[1,\infty]
, and any
t>0
,

\|e^{t\Delta}f\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq Ct^{-\beta/2-1/p}\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

where
C
depends on
\beta
and
p
.

There are similar estimates regarding the fractional Laplacian
\Lambda
on
\mathbb{T}
and the associated fractional heat semigroup.

Lemma A.2
.

Recall
\Lambda:=(-\Delta)^{1/2}_{\mathbb{T}}
.
Let
\{e^{-t\Lambda}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the semigroup associated with the fractional heat operator
\partial_{t}+\Lambda
on
\mathbb{T}
.
Let
f=f(s)
be defined on
\mathbb{T}
.
Assume
\gamma\in(0,1)
.
Then

(1)

\|\Lambda f\|_{{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}
,
where
C
depends on
\gamma
.

(2)

For any
t>0
,

\|e^{-t\Lambda}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}.

For any
\beta\geq 0
and
t>0
,

\|e^{-t\Lambda}f\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}\leq Ct^{-\beta}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})},

where
C
depends on
\beta
.

(3)

For
0\leq\alpha\leq\beta
, and
t>0
,

\|e^{-t\Lambda}f\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}\leq Ct^{\alpha-\beta}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}.

For
0\leq\alpha<\beta<\alpha+1
, and
t>0
,

\|e^{-t\Lambda}f-f\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq Ct^{\beta-\alpha}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}.

Here the constant
C
depends on
\alpha
and
\beta
.

We also have the following improved estimates.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
f=f(s)
be defined on
\mathbb{T}
.
For any
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
t>0
,

\big\|e^{-t\Lambda}f\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{2}{e^{t}+1}\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}.

Proof.

Note that

e^{-t\Lambda}f(s):=\int_{\mathbb{T}}P_{t}(s-s^{\prime})f(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime},

where

P_{t}(s):=\frac{1}{2\pi}\cdot\frac{\sinh t}{\cosh t-\cos s}\geq\frac{1}{2\pi}\cdot\frac{\sinh t}{1+\cosh t}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\big\|e^{-t\Lambda}f\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}=

\displaystyle\;\left\|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left[P_{t}(s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\pi}\cdot\frac{\sinh t}{1+\cosh t}\right]f^{\prime}(s-s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{\sinh t}{\cosh t-\cos s^{\prime}}-\frac{\sinh t}{1+\cosh t}\,ds^{\prime}\cdot\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\left[1-\frac{\sinh t}{1+\cosh t}\right]\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}=\frac{2}{e^{t}+1}\|f^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Similarly, with
\alpha\in(0,1]
, for distinct
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{T}
,

\displaystyle\;\left|e^{-t\Lambda}f^{\prime}(s_{1})-e^{-t\Lambda}f^{\prime}(s_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left[P_{t}(s^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\pi}\cdot\frac{\sinh t}{1+\cosh t}\right]\big(f^{\prime}(s_{1}-s^{\prime})-f^{\prime}(s_{2}-s^{\prime})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{\sinh t}{\cosh t-\cos s^{\prime}}-\frac{\sinh t}{1+\cosh t}\,ds^{\prime}\cdot\|f^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}|s_{1}-s_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{2}{e^{t}+1}\|f^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\alpha}}|s_{1}-s_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}}^{\alpha},

which implies the desired inequality.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Recall the operator
\mathcal{I}
was defined in (
2.21
), i.e., given
W=W(s,t)
on
\mathbb{T}\times[0,T]
, define
\mathcal{I}[W]
on
\mathbb{T}\times[0,T]
by

\mathcal{I}[W](t):=\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\,d\tau.

(1)

Assume that
\varphi:(0,+\infty)\to(0,+\infty)
is decreasing on
(0,+\infty)
and it is integrable on any bounded interval.
Then for
\gamma\in(0,1)
and any
t\in(0,T]
,

\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\frac{C}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(\tau)\,d\tau\cdot\sup_{\tau\in(0,T]}\varphi(\tau)^{-1}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})},

where
C
only depends on
\gamma
.

(2)

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
,
\beta\in(\alpha,1+\alpha]
,
\mu\in[0,1)
, and
t\in(0,T]
,

\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq Ct^{\beta-\alpha-\mu}\min\big(1,t^{\alpha-\beta}\big)\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\mu}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})},

where
C
depends on
\alpha
,
\beta
and
\mu
.

As a corollary of the above two estimates, for
0<\gamma\leq\beta<1
, it holds for any
t\in(0,T]
that

\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1}(\mathbb{T})}+t^{\gamma}\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\beta}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})},

where
C
depends on
\beta
and
\gamma
.

(3)

Assume
t_{1}\in(0,T)
.
Suppose that, for some
\delta>0
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

t\mapsto\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{C^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\mbox{ and }t\mapsto\|W(t)\|_{C^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\mbox{ are bounded in }[t_{1}-\delta,t_{1}+\delta],

and in addition,

\lim_{t\to t_{1}}\|W(t)-W(t_{1})\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}=0.

Then
\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})
is well-defined pointwise, and it holds pointwise that

\partial_{t}\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})+W(t_{1}).

Proof.

By Lemma
3.15
and Lemma
A.2
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{C}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(\tau)\,d\tau\left|\sup_{\tau\in(0,T)}(t-\tau)^{1-\gamma}\varphi(\tau)^{-1}\|\partial_{s}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right|^{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left|\sup_{\tau\in(0,T)}(t-\tau)^{2-\gamma}\varphi(\tau)^{-1}\|\partial_{s}^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}}\right|^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{C}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(\tau)\,d\tau\left|\sup_{\tau\in(0,T)}(t-\tau)^{1-\gamma}\cdot(t-\tau)^{-(1-\gamma)}\varphi(\tau)^{-1}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right|^{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\;\cdot\left|\sup_{\tau\in(0,T)}(t-\tau)^{2-\gamma}\cdot(t-\tau)^{-(2-\gamma)}\varphi(\tau)^{-1}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}\right|^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{C}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(\tau)\,d\tau\cdot\sup_{\tau\in(0,T]}\varphi(\tau)^{-1}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}.

This proves the first inequality.

Regarding the second claim, we apply Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.3
to find that, for
\alpha\in[0,1)
,
\beta\in(\alpha,1+\alpha]
, and
\mu\in[0,1)
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}[W](t)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\int_{0}^{t}\big\|e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\big\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{1,\alpha}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{8}(t-\tau)}(t-\tau)^{-(1+\alpha-\beta)}\tau^{-\mu}\cdot\tau^{\mu}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\left[e^{-\frac{t}{16}}t^{-(1+\alpha-\beta)+1-\mu}+\min\big(1,t^{-\alpha+\beta}\big)t^{-\mu}\right]\sup_{\tau\in(0,t]}\tau^{\mu}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\beta}(\mathbb{T})}.

This proves the desired estimate.

Finally, to prove the last claim, we need to justify that

\lim_{t_{2}\to t_{1}}\frac{\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})}{t_{2}-t_{1}}=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})+W(t_{1})\mbox{ in }C(\mathbb{T}).

By definition, for any
t_{1}<t_{2}
,

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-t_{1})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\,d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-\tau)\Lambda}W(t_{1})\,d\tau+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-\tau)\Lambda}\big[W(\tau)-W(t_{1})\big]\,d\tau+\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-t_{1})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1}).

Since
W(t_{1}),\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})\in C(\mathbb{T})
, one can readily show that, in the
C(\mathbb{T})
-topology,

\displaystyle\lim_{t_{2}\to t_{1}^{+}}\frac{1}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-\tau)\Lambda}W(t_{1})\,d\tau=W(t_{1}),

and

\lim_{t_{2}\to t_{1}^{+}}\frac{1}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-t_{1})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}-\frac{1}{4\delta}\int_{0}^{\delta}e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})\,d\tau=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1}).

Moreover,

\displaystyle\;\left\|\frac{1}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-\tau)\Lambda}\big[W(\tau)-W(t_{1})\big]\,d\tau\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|W(\tau)-W(t_{1})\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}\,d\tau\leq\sup_{\tau\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\|W(\tau)-W(t_{1})\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}.

Thanks to the time-continuity of
W
,

\displaystyle\;\lim_{t_{2}\to t_{1}^{+}}\frac{1}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-\tau)\Lambda}\big[W(\tau)-W(t_{1})\big]\,d\tau=0\mbox{ in }C(\mathbb{T}).

Therefore, it holds in the
C(\mathbb{T})
-topology that

\lim_{t_{2}\to t_{1}^{+}}\frac{\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})}{t_{2}-t_{1}}=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})+W(t_{1}).

On the other hand, for
t_{1}>t_{2}
, one similarly derives that

\displaystyle\;\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\,d\tau+\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-t_{2})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{2}-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\,d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-\tau)\Lambda}W(t_{1})\,d\tau+\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-t_{2})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})

\displaystyle\;+\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-\tau)\Lambda}\big[W(\tau)-W(t_{1})\big]\,d\tau+\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-t_{2})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)\big[\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})\big].

The first three terms can be handled as before; we omit the details.
It remains to show that

\lim_{t_{2}\to t_{1}^{-}}\frac{1}{t_{1}-t_{2}}\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-t_{2})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)\big[\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})\big]=0\mbox{ in }C(\mathbb{T}).

To that end, we first claim that
t\mapsto\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t)
is continuous in
C(\mathbb{T})
at
t=t_{1}
.
Indeed, for
\beta\in(0,\gamma)
, and
t_{1}>t_{2}
with
|t_{1}-t_{2}|<\delta\ll 1
,

\displaystyle\;\big\|\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})-\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})\big\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\|\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}\|e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-\tau)\Lambda}W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{T})}\,d\tau+C\big\|\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-t_{2})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})\big\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\beta}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}(t_{1}-\tau)^{-(1+\beta-\gamma)}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\,d\tau+C|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\gamma-\beta}\|\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\gamma-\beta}\left[\sup_{\tau\in[t_{1}-\delta,t_{1}]}\|W(\tau)\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+\|\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})\|_{\dot{C}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\right],

so the desired time-continuity follows from the assumptions.
Then we derive that

\displaystyle\;\left\|\frac{1}{t_{1}-t_{2}}\big(e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t_{1}-t_{2})\Lambda}-\mathrm{Id}\big)\big[\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})\big]\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\left\|\frac{1}{4(t_{1}-t_{2})}\int_{0}^{t_{1}-t_{2}}\Lambda e^{-\frac{\tau}{4}\Lambda}\big[\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})\big]\,d\tau\right\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{4(t_{1}-t_{2})}\int_{0}^{t_{1}-t_{2}}\big\|\Lambda\big[\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})\big]\big\|_{C(\mathbb{T})}\,d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{4}\big\|\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})-\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})\big\|_{C(\mathbb{T})},

which converges to
0
as
t_{2}\to t_{1}^{-}
.
Therefore, we can conclude that

\lim_{t_{2}\to t_{1}^{-}}\frac{\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})-\mathcal{I}[W](t_{2})}{t_{1}-t_{2}}=-\frac{1}{4}\Lambda\mathcal{I}[W](t_{1})+W(t_{1})\mbox{ in }C(\mathbb{T}),

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Some Auxiliary Calculus Results

We collect some useful auxiliary calculus results in the following lemma.

Lemma B.1
.

Suppose
\gamma\in(0,1)
.
Let
f=f(x)
be defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
X,X_{1},X_{2}:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Then

\displaystyle\|f\circ X\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}^{\gamma},

\displaystyle\|f\circ X\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|X\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},

\displaystyle\|f\circ X_{1}-f\circ X_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|X_{1}-X_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},

and

\displaystyle\;\|f\circ X_{1}-f\circ X_{2}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\|f\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|X_{1}-X_{2}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}+C\|f\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{1,\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|(X_{1}^{\prime},X_{2}^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})}^{\gamma}\|X_{1}-X_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}(\mathbb{T})},

where
C
only depends on
\gamma
.

Proof.

We only show the last estimate since the others are straightforward to verify.

For arbitrary
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{T}
,

\displaystyle\;|f\circ X_{1}(s_{1})-f\circ X_{2}(s_{1})-f\circ X_{1}(s_{2})+f\circ X_{2}(s_{2})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\bigg|\int_{0}^{1}(X_{1}(s_{1})-X_{2}(s_{1}))\cdot\nabla f\big(\theta X_{1}(s_{1})+(1-\theta)X_{2}(s_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\;\quad-(X_{1}(s_{2})-X_{2}(s_{2}))\cdot\nabla f\big(\theta X_{1}(s_{2})+(1-\theta)X_{2}(s_{2})\big)\,d\theta\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\int_{0}^{1}|X_{1}(s_{1})-X_{2}(s_{1})-X_{1}(s_{2})+X_{2}(s_{2})|\cdot\big|\nabla f\big(\theta X_{1}(s_{1})+(1-\theta)X_{2}(s_{1})\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\quad+|X_{1}(s_{2})-X_{2}(s_{2})|\cdot\big|\nabla f\big(\theta X_{1}(s_{1})+(1-\theta)X_{2}(s_{1})\big)-\nabla f\big(\theta X_{1}(s_{2})+(1-\theta)X_{2}(s_{2})\big)\big|\,d\theta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\int_{0}^{1}\|X_{1}-X_{2}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\gamma}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}

\displaystyle\;\quad+\|X_{1}-X_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}}\big|\theta X_{1}(s_{1})-\theta X_{1}(s_{2})+(1-\theta)X_{2}(s_{1})-(1-\theta)X_{2}(s_{2})\big|^{\gamma}\,d\theta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\|X_{1}-X_{2}\|_{\dot{C}_{s}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\gamma}\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+C\|X_{1}-X_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}\|\nabla f\|_{\dot{C}_{x}^{\gamma}}\|(X_{1}^{\prime},X_{2}^{\prime})\|_{L^{\infty}_{s}}^{\gamma}|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\gamma}.

This implies the desired bound.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of the Improved Estimates for the Nonlinear Term
g_{X}

In this section, we provide self-consistent justifications of Lemma
3.10
and Lemma
3.11
, which are improved estimates for the nonlinear terms
g_{X}
and
g_{X}-g_{Y}
arising in the Stokes flow field (see (
2.11
)).
Since their representations are purely Lagrangian, in what follows, we shall omit the subscript
s
in all the norm notations.

C.1.
Proof of Lemma
3.10

For
s\neq s^{\prime}
, denote

(C.1)

L(s,s^{\prime}):=\frac{X(s^{\prime})-X(s)}{s^{\prime}-s}.

We understand
s^{\prime}-s
as the usual subtraction on
\mathbb{R}
.
When
s^{\prime}\in[s-\frac{3}{2}\pi,s+\frac{3}{2}\pi]
, it is not difficult to show by the regularity and the well-stretched property of
X
that

(C.2)

C\lambda\leq|L(s,s^{\prime})|\leq\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad\lambda\leq\inf_{s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}}|X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\leq\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}},

and

(C.3)

|L(s,s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\leq\left|\frac{1}{s^{\prime}-s}\int_{s}^{s^{\prime}}X^{\prime}(\tau)-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\,d\tau\right|\leq C\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}.

Note that here we choose not to define the subtraction
s^{\prime}-s
in the modulo
2\pi
(i.e., making it range in
[-\pi,\pi)
) in order to allow some flexibility later in the Hölder estimate.

By the definition (
2.11
),

(C.4)

(g_{X})_{i}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X(s)-X(s^{\prime}))X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})+\frac{1}{4\pi}\cdot\frac{X_{i}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}{2\tan\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\,ds^{\prime}.

From (
1.17
), we can explicitly calculate that, for
x\neq 0
,

\partial_{k}G_{ij}(x)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\left(-\frac{\delta_{ij}x_{k}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{\delta_{ik}x_{j}+x_{i}\delta_{jk}}{|x|^{2}}-\frac{2x_{i}x_{j}x_{k}}{|x|^{4}}\right),

which is homogeneous of degree
-1
, and thus

(C.5)

\partial_{k}G_{ij}(x)x_{k}=-\frac{1}{4\pi}\delta_{ij}.

Hence,

(C.6)

\begin{split}(g_{X})_{i}(s)=&\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{s-s^{\prime}}\cdot\partial_{k}G_{ij}\left(\frac{X(s)-X(s^{\prime})}{s-s^{\prime}}\right)X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})+\frac{1}{4\pi}\cdot\frac{X_{i}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}{2\tan\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\,ds^{\prime}\\
=&\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{s-s^{\prime}}\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right]X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{s-s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2\tan\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\right)X_{i}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}.\end{split}

Here we may take the range of
s^{\prime}
to be any interval of length
2\pi
that contains, e.g.,
(s-\frac{\pi}{2},s+\frac{\pi}{2})
, and the value of the right-hand side does not depend on the choice of the interval.

One can readily show that, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

(C.7)

|\nabla^{k}G(x)|\leq C|x|^{-k},\quad|\nabla^{k}G(x)-\nabla^{k}G(y)|\leq C\min(|x|,|y|)^{-(k+1)}|x-y|.

Using this as well as (
C.2
) and (
C.3
),

\displaystyle\|g_{X}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{|s-s^{\prime}|}\cdot\lambda^{-2}|L(s,s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\,ds^{\prime}+C\int_{\mathbb{T}}|X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{|s-s^{\prime}|}\cdot\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\,ds^{\prime}+C\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}.

In the last line, we used the fact that
\lambda\leq\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}
.
This proves the
L^{\infty}
-bound.

Next we turn to the Hölder estimate for
g_{X}
.
We first rewrite (
C.4
) as (cf. (
2.9
) and (
2.11
))

(g_{X})_{i}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(X(s)-X(s^{\prime})\big)\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)+\frac{1}{4\pi}\cdot\frac{X_{i}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{i}^{\prime}(s)}{2\tan\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\,ds^{\prime}.

Note that the added terms actually vanish.
Using (
C.5
) and deriving as before, we obtain that

\displaystyle(g_{X})_{i}(s)=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{s-s^{\prime}}\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right]X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{s-s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2\tan\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\right)\big(X_{i}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{i}^{\prime}(s)\big)\,ds^{\prime}.

Again, we may interpret
\mathbb{T}
here to be an arbitrary interval on
\mathbb{R}
of length
2\pi
that contains, say,
(s-\frac{\pi}{2},s+\frac{\pi}{2})
.
In this way,
s-s^{\prime}
can be understood as the regular subtraction on
\mathbb{R}
, while the value of the integral does not depend on the choice of the interval.

In the case
\gamma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
, we would like to bound
g_{X}(s_{1})-g_{X}(s_{2})
for distinct
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{T}
.
Denote
r:=|s_{1}-s_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}}
.
Assume
s_{2}=s_{1}+r
.
Without loss of generality, we also assume
r\in(0,\frac{\pi}{4})
, as otherwise, the inequality
|g_{X}(s_{1})-g_{X}(s_{2})|\leq 2\|g_{X}\|_{L^{\infty}}
is enough for deriving the Hölder estimate.

Let
I:=(s_{1}-r,s_{2}+r)
.
We split
g_{X}(s_{1})-g_{X}(s_{2})
as

(C.8)

\begin{split}&\;(g_{X})_{i}(s_{1})-(g_{X})_{i}(s_{2})\\
=&\;\int_{I}\frac{1}{s_{1}-s^{\prime}}\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right]X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;-\int_{I}\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s_{2},s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right]X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{2})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+\int_{I^{c}}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}-s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}\right)\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right]\\
&\;\qquad\quad\cdot X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+\int_{I^{c}}\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s_{2},s^{\prime})\big)\right]X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+\int_{I^{c}}\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s_{2},s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right]X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{2})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}-s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2\tan\frac{s_{1}-s^{\prime}}{2}}-\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2\tan\frac{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}{2}}\right)\big(X_{i}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{i}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2\tan\frac{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}{2}}\right)\big(X_{i}^{\prime}(s_{2})-X_{i}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
=:&\;\sum_{m=1}^{7}\big(g_{m}[X]\big)_{i}.\end{split}

In all the integrals above, we choose to interpret
\mathbb{T}:=[s_{2}-\pi,s_{2}+\pi)
, and
I^{c}:=\mathbb{T}\setminus I
.
In what follows, we shall simply denote
g_{m}:=g_{m}[X]
whenever it incurs no confusion.

Arguing as before,

\displaystyle|g_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{s_{1}-r}^{s_{1}+2r}\frac{1}{|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|}\cdot\lambda^{-2}|L(s_{1},s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}\int_{s_{1}-r}^{s_{1}+2r}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{-1+2\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{2\gamma}\cdot\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}.

|g_{2}|
satisfies the same bound.

For any
s^{\prime}\in I^{c}=[s_{2}-\pi,s_{2}+\pi)\setminus(s_{2}-2r,s_{2}+r)
, it holds that

(C.9)

\frac{1}{2}|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|\leq|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|\leq 2|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|,\quad\left|\frac{1}{s_{1}-s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}\right|\leq\frac{C|s_{1}-s_{2}|}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{2}}.

Note that this is true because we chose the ranges of
s^{\prime}
in both the integrals of
g_{X}(s_{1})
and
g_{X}(s_{2})
to be
[s_{2}-\pi,s_{2}+\pi)
, and also because we understood
s_{1}-s^{\prime}
and
s_{2}-s^{\prime}
as the usual subtraction on
\mathbb{R}
.
Moreover, for any
s^{\prime}\in I^{c}
,

(C.10)

\begin{split}|L(s_{1},s^{\prime})-L(s_{2},s^{\prime})|=&\;\left|\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\partial_{s}L(s,s^{\prime})\,ds\right|=\left|\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\frac{-X^{\prime}(s)(s^{\prime}-s)+(X(s^{\prime})-X(s))}{(s^{\prime}-s)^{2}}\,ds\right|\\
\leq&\;C\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s-s^{\prime}|^{-1+\gamma}\,ds\leq C\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s_{2}||s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{-1+\gamma}.\end{split}

Hence,

\displaystyle|g_{3}|+|g_{4}|\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{I^{c}}\frac{|s_{1}-s_{2}|}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-2}|L(s_{1},s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+C\int_{I^{c}}\frac{1}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|}\cdot\lambda^{-2}|L(s_{1},s^{\prime})-L(s_{2},s^{\prime})|\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{I^{c}}\frac{|s_{1}-s_{2}|}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-2}|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+C\int_{I^{c}}\frac{1}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|}\cdot\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s_{2}||s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{-1+\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C|s_{1}-s_{2}|\cdot\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}\int_{I^{c}}|s^{\prime}-s_{2}|^{-2+2\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{2\gamma}\cdot\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}.

Since

\displaystyle\;\int_{s_{2}-\pi}^{s_{2}+\pi}\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s_{2},s^{\prime})\big)-\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{s_{2}-\pi}^{s_{2}+\pi}-\partial_{s^{\prime}}\big[G_{ij}\big(X(s_{2})-X(s^{\prime})\big)\big]+\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{1}{4\pi}\delta_{ij}\,ds^{\prime}=0,

we find that

(C.11)

(g_{5})_{i}=-\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{2})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\int_{I}\frac{1}{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}\big[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L(s_{2},s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big]X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime},

which can be handled as
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
:

\displaystyle|g_{5}|\leq

\displaystyle\;C|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\int_{I}\frac{1}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|}\cdot\lambda^{-2}\big|L(s_{2},s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big|\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{2\gamma}\cdot\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}.

Since
s^{-1}-(2\tan(s/2))^{-1}
is Lipschitz on
(-\frac{3\pi}{2},\frac{3\pi}{2})
,

|g_{6}|\leq C|s_{1}-s_{2}|\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Lastly, since
s^{\prime}
ranges in
[s_{2}-\pi,s_{2}+\pi)
,
g_{7}=0
due to the oddness of the integrand.

Summarizing all the estimates yields that, for
|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq\frac{\pi}{4}
,

|g_{X}(s_{1})-g_{X}(s_{2})|\leq C|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{2\gamma}\cdot\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2},

which implies that, for
\gamma\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
,

\|g_{X}\|_{\dot{C}^{2\gamma}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}.

In the case
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
, the
C^{1,2\gamma-1}
-estimate for
g_{X}
can be derived in a similar spirit.
Indeed, it has been proved in
[
38
, Proof of Proposition 3.8]
(also see
[
31
, Lemma 3.5]
) that

\displaystyle(g_{X})^{\prime}_{i}(s)=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}\partial_{s}\left[X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(X(s)-X(s^{\prime})\big)+\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\delta_{ij}}{2\tan\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\right]\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left[X_{l}^{\prime}(s)X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(X(s)-X(s^{\prime})\big)-\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\delta_{ij}}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\right]\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)\,ds^{\prime}.

Again, we may interpret
\mathbb{T}
to be an arbitrary interval of length
2\pi
in
\mathbb{R}
which contains, say,
(s-\frac{\pi}{2},s+\frac{\pi}{2})
;
in this case,
|s^{\prime}-s|\leq\frac{3}{2}\pi
.
We note that the integral does not depend on the choice of the interval.

Observe that
\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(x)
is homogeneous of degree
-2
, and by differentiating (
C.5
), for
x\neq 0
,

\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(x)x_{k}+\partial_{k}G_{ij}(x)\delta_{kl}=0,

which gives

\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(x)x_{k}x_{l}=-x_{l}\partial_{l}G_{ij}(x)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\delta_{ij}.

For
s\neq s^{\prime}
, denote

(C.12)

\tilde{L}(s,s^{\prime}):=\frac{X(s^{\prime})-X(s)}{2\sin\frac{s^{\prime}-s}{2}},

where we understand
s^{\prime}-s
as the usual subtraction on
\mathbb{R}
.
Then we derive as before to obtain

(C.13)

(g_{X})^{\prime}_{i}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\big[X_{l}^{\prime}(s)\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big]\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s)\big)\,ds^{\prime}.

For distinct
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{T}
, define
r
and
I
as before.
We still assume
s_{2}=s_{1}+r
and
r\in(0,\frac{\pi}{4})
.
Then

(C.14)

\begin{split}&\;(g_{X})^{\prime}_{i}(s_{1})-(g_{X})^{\prime}_{i}(s_{2})\\
=&\;\int_{I}\frac{X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s_{1}-s^{\prime}}{2}}\big[X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{1})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)-X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big]\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;-\int_{I}\frac{X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}{2}}\big[X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{2})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{2},s^{\prime})\big)-X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big]\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{2})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+\int_{I^{c}}\left(\frac{1}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s_{1}-s^{\prime}}{2}}-\frac{1}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}{2}}\right)X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\\
&\;\qquad\qquad\big[X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{1})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)-X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big]\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+\int_{I^{c}}\frac{X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}{2}}\big(X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{1})-X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{2})\big)\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+\int_{I^{c}}\frac{X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}{2}}X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{2})\big[\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{2},s^{\prime})\big)\big]\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+\int_{I^{c}}\frac{X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})}{4\sin^{2}\frac{s_{2}-s^{\prime}}{2}}\big[X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{2})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{2},s^{\prime})\big)-X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big]\big(X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{2})-X_{j}^{\prime}(s_{1})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
=:&\;\sum_{m=1}^{6}\big(\tilde{g}_{m}[X]\big)_{i}.\end{split}

Once again, in all the integrals above, we choose to interpret
\mathbb{T}:=[s_{2}-\pi,s_{2}+\pi)
, and
I^{c}:=\mathbb{T}\setminus I
.
For brevity, we shall simply denote
\tilde{g}_{m}:=\tilde{g}_{m}[X]
whenever it incurs no confusion.

Then we may estimate
\tilde{g}_{m}
as before, with in mind that
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
.
We only sketch them as follows.
First we note that, for
s^{\prime}\in[s-\frac{3}{2}\pi,s+\frac{3}{2}\pi]
with
s^{\prime}\neq s
,

c|s^{\prime}-s|\leq\left|2\sin\frac{s^{\prime}-s}{2}\right|\leq|s^{\prime}-s|,\quad c\lambda\leq|\tilde{L}(s,s^{\prime})|\leq C\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}

for some universal
c
and
C
, and

\displaystyle|\tilde{L}(s,s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle\;\left|\frac{s^{\prime}-s}{2\sin\frac{s^{\prime}-s}{2}}\right||L(s,s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|+\left|1-\frac{s^{\prime}-s}{2\sin\frac{s^{\prime}-s}{2}}\right||X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}+C\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}|s-s^{\prime}|\leq C\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}.

Then

(C.15)

\begin{split}&\;\big|X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{1})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)-X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big|\\
\leq&\;|X_{l}^{\prime}(s_{1})-X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\big|\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)\big|+|X_{l}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\big|\partial_{kl}G_{ij}\big(\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{kl}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big|\\
\leq&\;C|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\cdot\lambda^{-2}+C\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\cdot\lambda^{-3}|\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\\
\leq&\;C\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}.\end{split}

Hence,

\displaystyle|\tilde{g}_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{I}\frac{\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}}{|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|^{2}}\cdot\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{I}|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|^{-2+2\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{2\gamma-1}\cdot\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2},

and
|\tilde{g}_{2}|
satisfies the same estimate.
Moreover, for
s^{\prime}\in I^{c}
, by (
C.10
),

(C.16)

\begin{split}&\;|\tilde{L}(s_{1},s^{\prime})-\tilde{L}(s_{2},s^{\prime})|\\
\leq&\;\left|\frac{s^{\prime}-s_{1}}{2\sin\frac{s^{\prime}-s_{1}}{2}}\right||L(s_{1},s^{\prime})-L(s_{2},s^{\prime})|+\left|\frac{s^{\prime}-s_{1}}{2\sin\frac{s^{\prime}-s_{1}}{2}}-\frac{s^{\prime}-s_{2}}{2\sin\frac{s^{\prime}-s_{2}}{2}}\right||L(s_{2},s^{\prime})|\\
\leq&\;C\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s_{2}||s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{-1+\gamma}+C|s_{1}-s_{2}|\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\\
\leq&\;C\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s_{2}||s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{-1+\gamma},\end{split}

so we obtain that

\displaystyle\;|\tilde{g}_{3}|+|\tilde{g}_{4}|+|\tilde{g}_{5}|+|\tilde{g}_{6}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{I^{c}}\frac{|s_{1}-s_{2}|}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{3}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\cdot\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+C\int_{I^{c}}\frac{1}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\cdot|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\gamma}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\cdot\lambda^{-2}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+C\int_{I^{c}}\frac{1}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\cdot\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s_{2}||s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{-1+\gamma}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s^{\prime}-s_{1}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;+C\int_{I^{c}}\frac{1}{|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{2}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\cdot\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{2}-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\cdot\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{\gamma}\,ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C|s_{1}-s_{2}|^{2\gamma-1}\cdot\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}.

Therefore, when
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
,

\|g_{X}\|_{\dot{C}^{1,2\gamma-1}(\mathbb{T})}\leq C\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
3.10
.

C.2.
Proof of Lemma
3.11

Similar to (
C.1
), we denote for
s\neq s^{\prime}
that

L_{X}(s,s^{\prime}):=\frac{X(s^{\prime})-X(s)}{s^{\prime}-s},\quad L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime}):=\frac{Y(s^{\prime})-Y(s)}{s^{\prime}-s}.

Thanks to (
C.6
),

(C.17)

\begin{split}&\;(g_{X})_{i}(s)-(g_{Y})_{i}(s)\\
=&\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{s-s^{\prime}}\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L_{X}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right.\\
&\;\qquad\qquad\quad\left.-\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})\big)+\partial_{k}G_{ij}(Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right]X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{s-s^{\prime}}\left[\partial_{k}G_{ij}\big(L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\partial_{k}G_{ij}(Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\right]\big(X_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})X_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-Y_{k}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})Y_{j}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big)\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;-\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\frac{1}{s-s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2\tan\frac{s-s^{\prime}}{2}}\right)\big(X_{i}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})-Y_{i}^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\big)\,ds^{\prime}.\end{split}

Again, we understand
s^{\prime}-s
as the usual subtraction on
\mathbb{R}
, and
\mathbb{T}
can be taken to be an arbitrary interval of length
2\pi
in
\mathbb{R}
that contains, e.g.,
(s-\frac{\pi}{2},s+\frac{\pi}{2})
.

We first claim that, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

(C.18)

\begin{split}&\;\big|\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{X}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\nabla^{k}G(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))-\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})\big)+\nabla^{k}G(Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big|\\
\leq&\;C|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\Big[\lambda^{-(k+1)}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}+\lambda^{-(k+2)}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}+\|Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\big)\Big].\end{split}

Indeed, if
\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\geq\lambda/2
, we simply use (
C.2
), (
C.3
), and (
C.7
) to derive that

\displaystyle\;\big|\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{X}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\nabla^{k}G(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))-\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})\big)+\nabla^{k}G(Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\big|\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{X}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\nabla^{k}G(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big|+\big|\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\nabla^{k}G(Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-(k+1)}|L_{X}(s,s^{\prime})-X^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|+C\lambda^{-(k+1)}|L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})-Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-(k+1)}\big(\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}+\|Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\big)|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-(k+2)}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}+\|Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\big)|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}.

Otherwise, if
\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\lambda/2
, we let
Z(s;\theta):=\theta X(s)+(1-\theta)Y(s)
, where
\theta\in[0,1]
.
Then

\displaystyle\;\big|\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{X}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\nabla^{k}G(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))-\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})\big)+\nabla^{k}G(Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\left|\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\theta}\left[\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{Z(\cdot;\theta)}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\nabla^{k}G(Z^{\prime}(s^{\prime};\theta))\right]d\theta\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\int_{0}^{1}\left|\frac{(X-Y)(s)-(X-Y)(s^{\prime})}{s-s^{\prime}}\cdot\nabla^{k+1}G\big(L_{Z(\cdot;\theta)}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-(X-Y)^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\cdot\nabla^{k+1}G(Z^{\prime}(s^{\prime};\theta))\right|d\theta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\int_{0}^{1}\left|\frac{(X-Y)(s)-(X-Y)(s^{\prime})}{s-s^{\prime}}-(X-Y)^{\prime}(s^{\prime})\right|\big|\nabla^{k+1}G\big(L_{Z(\cdot;\theta)}(s,s^{\prime})\big)\big|

\displaystyle\;\qquad+|(X-Y)^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|\big|\nabla^{k+1}G\big(L_{Z(\cdot;\theta)}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\nabla^{k+1}G(Z^{\prime}(s^{\prime};\theta))\big|\,d\theta.

Since

\inf_{s\in\mathbb{T}}|Z^{\prime}(s)|\geq|Z(\cdot;\theta)|_{*}\geq|Y|_{*}-\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\geq\lambda/2,

by (
C.2
), (
C.3
), and (
C.7
),

\displaystyle\;\big|\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{X}(s,s^{\prime})\big)-\nabla^{k}G(X^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))-\nabla^{k}G\big(L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})\big)+\nabla^{k}G(Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime}))\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\int_{0}^{1}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}\cdot\big|L_{Z(\cdot;\theta)}(s,s^{\prime})\big|^{-(k+1)}

\displaystyle\;\qquad+\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\cdot\lambda^{-(k+2)}\big|L_{Z(\cdot;\theta)}(s,s^{\prime})-Z^{\prime}(s^{\prime};\theta)\big|\,d\theta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;C\lambda^{-(k+1)}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}+C\lambda^{-(k+2)}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}+\|Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\big)|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}.

This proves (
C.18
).

Now combining (
C.18
) with (
C.17
), and also using (
C.2
), (
C.3
), and (
C.7
), we find that

(C.19)

\begin{split}&\;\|g_{X}-g_{Y}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\\
\leq&\;C\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{|s-s^{\prime}|^{\gamma}}{|s-s^{\prime}|}\Big[\lambda^{-2}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}+\lambda^{-3}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(\|X^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}+\|Y^{\prime}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}}\big)\Big]\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+C\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{|s-s^{\prime}|}\cdot\lambda^{-2}|L_{Y}(s,s^{\prime})-Y^{\prime}(s^{\prime})|(\|X^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}})\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,ds^{\prime}\\
&\;+C\int_{\mathbb{T}}\|X^{\prime}-Y^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,ds^{\prime}\\
